) is commutative. To show that the triangle on the right end
of (
3.15
) is commutative, we look at the diagram

(3.16)

\textstyle{\Omega^{2}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{C}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Omega^{2}_{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{res}}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{C}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{res}}_{\mathfrak{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{C}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{C}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,\Omega^{2}_{K,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{2}}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{x}(S,\Omega^{2}_{S,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({\mathfrak{f}},\Omega^{1}_{{\mathfrak{f}},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({\mathbb{F}}_{q},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p),}

where
\partial_{1}
and
\partial_{2}
are Kato’s residue isomorphisms
(cf.
[
20
, § 3, Prop. 1]
). Since
{\rm Res}_{K}={\operatorname{tr}}\circ{\operatorname{res}}_{\mathfrak{f}}\circ{\operatorname{res}}_{K}
and
{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{K}=\partial_{1}\circ\partial_{2}
, the commutativity of
the triangle on the right end of (
3.15
) is equivalent to that
of the composite outer trapezium in (
3.16
).
However, the composition of the first two squares in (
3.16
)
is commutative by
[
20
, § 1.6, Lem. 13]
, and the same proof works
verbatim for the commutativity of the third square too. It remains to observe that
the triangle on the right end of (
3.16
) commutes. But this
is clear because

\frac{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}{(1-C)(Z{\mathbb{F}}_{q})}=\frac{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}{(1-\overline{F})({\mathbb{F}}_{q})}
and the sequence

{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\xrightarrow{1-\overline{F}}{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{tr}}}{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p\to 0
is
exact.
∎

The upshot of what we have shown above is the following.

Proposition 3.6
.

For every
n\geq 0
, the map
\rho^{n+1}_{K,1}\colon\kappa^{1}_{2}(A|n+1)\to\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}\right)^{\vee}
admits a
factorization

\kappa^{1}_{2}(A|n+1)\xrightarrow{\cong}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}\right)^{\star}\hookrightarrow\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}\right)^{\vee},

where the first dual is taken with respect to the adic topology.
In particular,
\rho^{n+1}_{K,1}
is injective.

Proof.

Combine Lemma
3.2
, (
3.12
) and Lemma
3.5
.
∎

4.
Reciprocity for 2-local fields II

In this section, we shall complete the proof of Theorem
1.4
.
We let
K
be a Henselian discrete valuation field of
characteristic
p>0
whose residue field
{\mathfrak{f}}
is a local field.
Let
A
denote the ring of integers of
K
and
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\pi)
the maximal ideal of

A
. We shall maintain the notations of §
2.4
.

4.1.
Kernel of
\rho_{K}

The following two results will prove part (1) of Theorem
1.4
, and also the
divisibility of
{\rm Ker}(\rho_{K})
.

Theorem 4.1
.

For every pair of integers
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
, the map

\rho^{n}_{K,m}\colon\kappa^{m}_{2}(A|n)\to G^{(n)}_{K,m}

is injective.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
2.3
and Corollary
2.9
, we can assume
that
K
is complete.
Since
\kappa^{m}_{2}(A|0)=\kappa^{m}_{2}(A|1)
by Lemma
2.2
and

G^{(0)}_{K,m}=G^{(1)}_{K,m}
by (
2.9
), we can assume
that
n\geq 1
. We shall now prove the theorem by induction on
m
.
The base case
m=1
follows from Proposition
3.6
.
For
m\geq 2
, we let
n^{\prime}=\lceil n/p\rceil
and
look at the commutative diagram

(4.1)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\kappa^{m-1}_{2}(A|n^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{n^{\prime}}_{K,m-1}}
\textstyle{\kappa^{m}_{2}(A|n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{n}_{K,m}}
\textstyle{\kappa^{1}_{2}(A|n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{n}_{K,1}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{(n^{\prime})}_{K,m-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{(n)}_{K,m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{G^{(n)}_{K,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where the unmarked arrows are the canonical inclusions and surjections.

The top row in (
4.1
)
is exact by
[
17
, Thm. 1.1.7]
. The exactness of the bottom
row follows (via the Pontryagin duality)
by comparing the exact sequence

(4.2)

0\to H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})\xrightarrow{(p^{m-1})^{*}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\xrightarrow{R^{*}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m-1}})\to 0

with the bottom row of (
2.11
) and using Proposition
2.8
.
Note here that
\lceil n/p\rceil=\lfloor{(n-1)}/p\rfloor+1
.
Since the left and the right vertical arrows in (
4.1
) are
injective by induction on
m
, we are done.
∎

Corollary 4.2
.

For every integer
q\geq 1
, the map

{\rho_{K}}\colon{K_{2}(K)}/q\to{G_{K}}/q
is injective. Equivalently, the map

{\rho_{A}}\colon{K_{2}(A)}/q\to{G^{(0)}_{K}}/q
is injective.

Proof.

The two assertions are equivalent by Lemma
2.11
.
To prove the result for
K
, we can assume
K
to be complete in view of
Lemma
2.3
and Corollary
2.9
.
If
q=p^{m}
, the claim is the
n=0
case
of Theorem
4.1
. If
p\nmid q
, then the Merkurjev-Suslin isomorphism

{\rm NR}_{K}\colon{K_{2}(K)}/q\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/q(2))
and
the Poincaré duality for the étale cohomology of
K

(cf.
[
36
, Thm. I.2.17]
or
[
29
]
)
imply that
{\rho_{K}}/q
is in fact an isomorphism.
∎

The following result recovers a theorem of Fesenko
[
4
]
.

Corollary 4.3
.

{\rm Ker}(\rho_{K})
is the maximal divisible subgroup of
K_{2}(K)
.

Proof.

Combine Lemma
2.12
, Corollary
4.2
and
Proposition
4.8
.
∎

4.2.
Reciprocity map for
\varpi^{m}_{2}(A|n)

Recall from (
2.2
) that
\varpi^{m}_{2}(A|n)
is the cokernel of the
canonical inclusion
\kappa^{m}_{2}(A|n)\hookrightarrow\kappa^{m}_{2}(A|0)
for
n\geq 0
.
Since
\left\{\rho^{n}_{K,m}\right\}_{n\geq 0}\colon\left\{\kappa^{m}_{2}(R|n)\right\}_{n\geq 0}\to\left\{\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{n-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\vee}\right\}_{n\geq 0}
constitute a
morphism between two pro-abelian groups as we vary
n
, taking the
quotients of the transition maps of these pro-abelian groups yields
a reciprocity homomorphism

(4.3)

\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m}\colon\varpi^{m}_{2}(R|n)\to\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\vee}.

Our aim in this subsection is to study this map as an application of the results of
§
3
. We shall use the results of this subsection in the
later parts of this paper.

We first consider a special case. We fix an integer
n\geq 0
and assume that
K
is
complete. We look at the commutative diagram

(4.4)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{A(n)}{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{1}}
\textstyle{\frac{K}{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{1}}
\textstyle{\frac{K}{A(n)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{1}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}_{n}}
\textstyle{\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}_{n}}
\textstyle{\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

in which
\alpha_{n}
(resp.
\alpha^{\prime}_{n}
) and
\beta_{n}
(resp.
\beta^{\prime}_{n}
)
are canonical inclusion and surjection.

Since the adic topology of
H^{1}_{\mathfrak{m}}(A(n))
coincides with the quotient topology
under the canonical surjection
H^{1}_{\mathfrak{m}}(A(0))\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathfrak{m}}(A(n))
, it follows
that the adic topology of

{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(S,{\mathcal{F}}_{n})\cong\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}

(cf. (
3.11
)) coincides with the quotient (via
\beta^{\prime}_{n}
)
of the adic topology of
\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}
.
We endow
\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}
with the
quotient of the adic topology of
\frac{A(n)}{A}
. It is clear that all arrows in
the above diagram are continuous.

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists a continuous homomorphism
\gamma^{\prime}_{n}\colon\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\to\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}
such that

\gamma^{\prime}_{n}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{n}={\operatorname{id}}
.

Proof.

We let
\gamma_{n}\colon\frac{K}{A}\to\frac{A(n)}{A}
be the projection
map
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\infty}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}{\mathfrak{f}}\pi^{-i}\to\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}{\mathfrak{f}}\pi^{-i}
so that

\gamma_{n}\circ\alpha_{n}={\operatorname{id}}
. It is clear that

\gamma_{n}
is continuous and
\overline{F}\circ\gamma_{\lfloor n/p\rfloor}=\gamma_{n}\circ\overline{F}
. Taking quotients by the images of
(1-\overline{F})

and using Lemma
2.7
,
we get that
\gamma_{n}
descends to the map
\gamma^{\prime}_{n}
, as desired.
∎

Using Lemma
4.4
, we get that the bottom row in the commutative
diagram

(4.5)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\kappa^{1}_{2}(A|n+1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{n+1}_{K,1}}
\textstyle{\kappa^{1}_{2}(A|1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{1}_{K,1}}
\textstyle{\varpi^{1}_{2}(A|n+1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\rho}^{n+1}_{K,1}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\right)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\right)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\right)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is exact, where the duals are taken with respect to the adic topologies.
The top row is exact by (
2.2
).
It follows from Lemma
3.2
, (
3.12
)
and Lemma
3.5
that the left and the middle vertical arrows are
bijective. It follows that
\widetilde{\rho}^{n+1}_{K,1}
is bijective.

If
K
is not necessarily complete, we can use
Lemma
2.3
and Corollary
2.9
to make sense of the adic
topology on
\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}
and
conclude the following.

Corollary 4.5
.

The reciprocity homomorphism

\widetilde{\rho}^{n+1}_{K,1}\colon\varpi^{1}_{2}(A|n+1)\to\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\right)^{\star}

is bijective.

We generalize the above result as follows.

Corollary 4.6
.

For
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
, the map
\rho^{n}_{K,m}

induces a monomorphism

\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m}\colon\varpi^{m}_{2}(A|n)\hookrightarrow\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\star},

where the dual on the right is taken with respect to the discrete topology.

Proof.

We can assume that
K
is complete in view of
Lemma
2.3
and Corollary
2.9
.
We can also assume that
n\geq 1
else both sides of
\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m}
are zero.
We now let
n^{\prime}=\lfloor n/p\rfloor
and look at the commutative diagram

(4.6)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varpi^{m-1}_{2}(A|n^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m-1}}
\textstyle{\varpi^{m}_{2}(A|n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m}}
\textstyle{\varpi^{1}_{2}(A|n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,1}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n^{\prime}-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m-1}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m-1}})}\right)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\right)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

The exactness of the top row follows by comparing the top row of
(
4.1
) with the similar exact sequence for
n=0
.
The exactness of the bottom row follows by comparing the bottom row of
(
2.11
) with (
4.2
) (with
K
replaced by
A
).
The left and the right vertical arrows in (
4.6
) are injective
by induction on
m
. It follows that
\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m}
is injective.
∎

A stronger claim is true about the dual of
\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m}
which we can
easily deduce from what Kato already showed about
\rho^{\star}_{K}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

For
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
, the map

(\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m})^{\star}\colon\frac{{\rm fil}_{n-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\to(\varpi^{m}_{2}(A|n))^{\star}

is an isomorphism, where the dual on the right
is taken with respect to the Kato topology.

Proof.

We can assume that
K
is complete.
We consider the commutative diagram

(4.7)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{0}_{K,m})^{\star}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({{\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}}/{p^{m}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({K_{2}(K)}/{p^{m}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({K_{2}(A)}/{p^{m}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where the vertical arrows are the duals of the reciprocity maps.
The bottom row is exact by Remark
2.6
.
The left vertical arrow is well known to be bijective.
The middle vertical arrow is bijective
by
[
19
, § 8, Thm. 2]
and
[
20
, § 3, Rem. 4]
.
It follows that the right vertical arrow is bijective.
In particular,
(\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m})^{\star}
is injective.

To prove that
(\widetilde{\rho}^{n}_{K,m})^{\star}
is surjective,
let
\chi\in(\varpi^{m}_{2}(A|n))^{\star}
. The surjectivity of

(\rho^{0}_{K,m})^{\star}
implies that there exists an element
\chi^{\prime}\in\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}
such that

(\rho^{0}_{K,m})^{\star}(\chi^{\prime})=\chi
. We only have to show that

\chi^{\prime}\in\frac{{\rm fil}_{n-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}
. But this follows at from

[
23
, Rem. 6.6]
.
∎

4.3.
Cokernel of
\rho_{K}

We shall now prove the remaining parts of Theorem
1.4
.
We fix an integer
n
not divisible by
p
.
We let
A={\rm Image}(\rho_{K})
and
B={\rm Coker}(\rho_{K})
. We also let

M=\frac{K_{2}({K})}{{\rm fil}_{1}K_{2}({K})},\ N=\frac{{\rm fil}_{0}K_{2}(K)}{{\rm fil}_{1}K_{2}(K)},\ M^{\prime}=\frac{G_{K}}{G^{(1)}_{K}}\ \mbox{and}\ N^{\prime}=\frac{G^{(0)}_{K}}{G^{(1)}_{K}}.

Proposition 4.8
.

The map
~{}_{n}K_{2}(K)\to~_{n}G_{K}
is surjective.

Proof.

We consider the commutative diagram

(4.8)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}{\rm fil}_{1}K_{2}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}K_{2}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{K}}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}_{K}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}G_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}M^{\prime}.}

The top row of this diagram is exact because
{\rm fil}_{1}K_{2}(K)
is
n
-divisible
(cf.
[
9
, Lem. 5.9]
and its proof) and the bottom horizontal arrow
is an isomorphism because
G^{(1)}_{K^{\prime}}
is a pro-
p
group and hence uniquely

n
-divisible. We thus have to show that
\rho^{\prime}_{K}
is surjective.
By Lemma
2.3
and Corollary
2.9
, we can assume that

K
is complete.

We now look at the commutative diagram of exact sequences

(4.9)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{K}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}_{K}}
\textstyle{K_{1}({\mathfrak{f}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\mathfrak{f}}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{\mathfrak{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where the vertical arrows are induced by the reciprocity maps.
One knows that the top row is split exact (cf. the proof of Lemma
2.5
).

We thus get a commutative diagram with exact rows

(4.10)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{K}}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}_{K}}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}K_{1}({\mathfrak{f}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\mathfrak{f}}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}N^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}M^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{~{}_{n}G_{\mathfrak{f}}.}

Since the right vertical arrow is an isomorphism, we get that the map

~{}_{n}M^{\prime}\to~_{n}G_{\mathfrak{f}}
in the bottom row is surjective.
Since
N\cong{\rm div}(N)\bigoplus N_{0}
and
\alpha_{K}({{\rm div}(N)})=0
, where

{\rm div}(N)
is the maximal divisible subgroup of
N
, it suffices to show that
the map
\alpha_{K}\colon~_{n}N_{0}\to~_{n}N^{\prime}
is surjective.
We shall show that
\alpha_{K}\colon N_{0}\to N^{\prime}
is in fact bijective.

By Lemma
2.4
, the two rows of the commutative
diagram

(4.11)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}({\mathfrak{f}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(G^{(0)}_{K})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{1}({\mathfrak{f}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{2}(K)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\rm fil}_{0}K_{2}(K))^{\star}}

are exact with respect to the Kato and adic topologies.
Since the left and the middle vertical arrows are bijective
(cf.
[
20
, § 3.5]
), it follows that the right vertical arrow is
injective. Combining this with the commutative diagram

(4.12)

\textstyle{N^{\prime\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha_{K})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{(G^{(0)}_{K})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\rm fil}_{0}K_{2}(K))^{\star},}

we conclude that
(\alpha_{K})^{\vee}\colon N^{\prime\star}\to N_{0}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\cong}N^{\vee}
is injective.

On the other hand,

N^{\prime}\cong\mu_{\infty}({\mathfrak{f}})\cong N_{0}
are finite groups by
[
18
, Thm. 3]

and
[
42
]
, where
\mu_{\infty}(R)=R^{\times}_{\rm tor}
for any ring
R
.
It follows that
(\alpha_{K})^{\vee}\colon N^{\prime\vee}\to N_{0}^{\vee}
is an injective homomorphism between finite groups of the same
cardinality. In particular, it is bijective. Taking the dual, we get that

\alpha_{K}\colon N_{0}\to N^{\prime}
is bijective. This concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.9
.

The cokernel of
\rho_{K}\colon K_{2}(K)\to G_{K}
is uniquely
n
-divisible.

Proof.

It follows from the Merkurjev-Suslin isomorphism
{K_{2}(K)}/n\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/n(2))
, Lemma
2.3
and

[
29
, Thm. 3.3]
(see also
[
36
, Thm. 2.17]
) that the map

{\rho_{K}}\colon{K_{2}(K)}/n\to{G_{K}}/n
is bijective.
Combining this with Proposition
4.8
and
the exact sequence

(4.13)

0\to~_{n}A\to~_{n}G_{K}\to~_{n}B\to A/n\to{G_{K}}/n\to B/n\to 0,

we conclude the proof.
∎

We end this section with the following result.

Proposition 4.10
.

The cokernel of
\rho_{K}\colon K_{2}(K)\to G_{K}
has no
p
-torsion but it is
not
p
-divisible.

Proof.

The first claim of the proposition follows directly from Lemma
2.12

and Corollary
4.2
.
To prove the second claim, it suffices to show, using the bijectivity of

\rho_{\mathfrak{f}}
with finite coeffcients, Lemma
2.2
and the diagram
(
4.5
) (with
(-)^{\star}
in place of
(-)^{\vee}
in the
bottom row), that

\widetilde{\rho}^{2}_{K,1}\colon\varpi^{1}_{2}(A|2)\to\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}\right)^{\vee}
is
not surjective.
To show the latter claim, we write

F=\frac{{\rm fil}_{1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p})}
.
By Corollary
4.5
and
[
19
, § 2, Prop. 1]
,
we get that
{\mathfrak{f}}\xrightarrow{\cong}\varpi^{1}_{2}(A|2)\xrightarrow{\cong}F^{\star}
. On the other hand, it follows from

[
23
, Cor. 3.3]
that
F\cong{\mathfrak{f}}
. We are now done because an
elementary cardinality argument tells us that no homomorphism

{\mathfrak{f}}\to{\mathfrak{f}}^{\vee}
can be surjective.
∎

5.
Reciprocity map for curves

Our objective in the rest of this paper is to study the class field theory of
smooth curves over local fields of positive characteristics.
For all of this section, we fix a local field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.
We also fix a projective curve
X
over
k
which is normal and connected.
We let
K
denote the function field of
X
. By a modulus pair, we shall mean
a pair
(X,D)
, where
D\subset X
is an effective Cartier divisor.

For
x\in X_{(0)}
, we let
A_{x}={\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
with the maximal ideal

{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
. We shall write
X_{x}={\rm Spec\,}(A_{x}),\ X^{h}_{x}={\rm Spec\,}(A^{h}_{x})
and

\widehat{X}_{x}={\rm Spec\,}(\widehat{A}_{x})
. We let
K^{h}_{x}
(resp.
\widehat{K}_{x}
) denote the
quotient field of
A^{h}_{x}
(resp.
\widehat{A}_{x}
). Given an effective Weil divisor

D=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}[x_{i}]\in{\rm Div}(X)
with complement
U
and

x\in X_{(0)}
, we shall let
n_{x}=n_{i}
if
x=x_{i}
for some

i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}
and
n_{x}=0
if
x\notin D
.
We let

(5.1)

{\rm fil}_{D}K_{2}(K)={\underset{x\in D}{\bigcap}}K_{2}(A_{x}|{\mathfrak{m}}^{n_{x}}_{x}),\ F_{0}(U)={\underset{x\in U}{\bigoplus}}{\mathbb{Z}},\ F_{1}(U)={\underset{x\in U}{\bigoplus}}k(x)^{\times},\

F_{2}(U)={\underset{x\notin U}{\bigoplus}}{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})},\ F^{c}_{2}(U)={\underset{x\notin U}{\bigoplus}}{K_{2}(\widehat{K}_{x})}\ \ \mbox{and}

F_{2}(X|D)={\underset{x\in D}{\bigoplus}}\frac{K_{2}({K}^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}K_{2}({K}^{h}_{x})}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\underset{x\in D}{\bigoplus}}\frac{K_{2}(\widehat{K}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}K_{2}(\widehat{K}_{x})},

where the isomorphism on the right follows from Lemma
2.3
.

5.1.
The idele class groups

Let
D\subset X
be an effective divisor with complement
U
.
Recall (cf.
[
22
]
) that there is a tame symbol map

\partial_{U}\colon K_{n+1}(K)\to{\underset{x\in U_{(0)}}{\bigoplus}}K_{n}(k(x))
.
We let
\vartheta_{X|D}
denote the composition of the diagonal map

K_{n}(K)\xrightarrow{\vartheta_{U}}{\underset{x\in D}{\bigoplus}}K_{n}({K}^{h}_{x})
with the quotient map

{\underset{x\in D}{\bigoplus}}K_{n}({K}^{h}_{x})\twoheadrightarrow{\underset{x\in D}{\bigoplus}}\frac{K_{n}({K}^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}K_{n}({K}^{h}_{x})}
.

Definition 5.1
.

(
cf.
[
13
]
)

The idele class group
C(X,D)
of the modulus pair
(X,D)
is defined to be the
cokernel of the map
K_{2}(K)\xrightarrow{(\partial_{U},\vartheta_{X|D})}F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F_{2}(X|D)
. We let
C(X)=C(X,\emptyset)
.

For each
x\in X_{(0)}
, we endow
\frac{K_{2}({K}^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{n}K_{2}({K}^{h}_{x})}
with
the quotient of the Kato topology of
K_{2}({K}^{h}_{x})
.
The Kato topology of
F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F_{2}(X|D)
is the direct sum
topology with respect to the Kato topology on each factor.
We let
C(X,D)
be endowed with the quotient of the topology of

F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F_{2}(X|D)
. This makes
C(X,D)
into a topological
abelian group.

The following description of
C(X,D)
follows from
[
9
, Lem. 5.6]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

There is an exact sequence

{\rm fil}_{D}K_{2}(K)\xrightarrow{\partial_{U}}F_{1}(U)\to C(X,D)\to 0.

Let
{\rm CH}^{q}(X|D,p)
(resp.
H^{q}_{\tau}(X|D,{\mathbb{Z}}(p))
)
denote the higher Chow group (resp. motivic cohomology with respect to the
topology
\tau
) of the modulus pair
(X,D)
(cf.
[
39
, § 1]
).

Corollary 5.3
.

For
\tau\in\{{\rm zar},{\rm nis}\}
, we have the following.

(1)

There are canonical maps

{\rm CH}^{2}(X|D,1)\twoheadrightarrow H^{3}_{\tau}(X|D,{\mathbb{Z}}(2))\xrightarrow{\cong}C(X,D)\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{1}_{\tau}(X,{\mathcal{K}}_{2,X|D}).

(2)

The canonical maps

{C(X,D)}/{p^{m}}\to H^{1}_{\tau}(X,{{\mathcal{K}}_{2,X|D}}/{p^{m}})\to H^{1}_{\tau}(X,{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X|D})

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

The left arrow in (1) is the well known canonical map.
The claim that
the right arrow is an isomorphism is an easy consequence of
Lemma
5.2
using the support spectral sequence for

H^{1}_{\tau}(X,{\mathcal{K}}_{2,X|D})
.
The claim that the composition of the middle and the right arrows is an
isomorphism is shown in
[
39
, Thm. 1]
. Finally, it is
easy to check that the composition of the left and the middle arrows is
surjective because it is induced by the norm map

z^{2}(X|D,1)\to F_{1}(X\setminus D)
, which has a section since
k
is infinite.
The proof of (2) is identical to that of (1).
∎

Remark 5.4
.

It follows from Lemma
2.1
and
[
9
, Prop. 6.5]
that
the maps in Corollary
5.3
induce an isomorphism between the pro-abelian
groups (compare with
[
39
, Thm. 2]
)

\Phi_{X}\colon\{{\rm CH}^{2}(X|nD,1)\}_{n\geq 1}\xrightarrow{\cong}\{C(X,nD)\}_{n\geq 1}.

Definition 5.5
.

(
cf.
[
13
, defn. 3.2]
)

The idele class group
C(U)
of an open curve
U\subset X
is defined to be the
cokernel of the canonical map
K_{2}(K)\xrightarrow{(\partial_{U},\vartheta_{U})}F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F_{2}(U)
.
We let
C^{c}(U)
be the cokernel of the canonical map
K_{2}(K)\xrightarrow{(\partial_{U},\vartheta_{U})}F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F^{c}_{2}(U)
.
The class groups
C(U)
and
C^{c}(U)
are topological abelian groups endowed with
the quotients of the Kato topology of

F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F_{2}(U)
and
F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F^{c}_{2}(U)
,
respectively.
We let
\widehat{C}(U)={\varprojlim}_{D}\ C(X,D)
with the
inverse limit topology, where the limit is taken over all effective divisors
having support disjoint from
U
.

Note that the above class groups depend only on
U
since
the latter admits a unique regular compactification up to isomorphism.
There are continuous homomorphisms

(5.2)

{C(U)}/n\hookrightarrow{C^{c}(U)}/n\xrightarrow{\theta_{U}}{\widehat{C}(U)}/n\xrightarrow{\phi_{X|D}}{C(X,D)}/n

for every integer
n\geq 0
and effective divisor
D
supported away from
U
.
The first arrow is injective because

{F_{2}(U)}/n\hookrightarrow{F^{c}_{2}(U)}/n
by Lemma
2.3
.
All arrows in this sequence are isomorphisms if
p\nmid n
.

5.2.
Étale idele class group

Let
D=\sum_{x}n_{x}[x]\in{\rm Div}(X)
be an effective divisor with complement
U
.
We fix an integer
m\geq 1
. We let
C_{m}(X,D)={C(X,D)}/{p^{m}}

and
C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D)=H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X|D})
. We let

T_{m}(X)
be the cokernel of the canonical map
C_{m}(X)\to C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X)
. It follows
from the Leray spectral sequence associated to the morphism of sites

\epsilon\colon X_{\text{\'{e}t}}\to X_{\rm nis}
that
T_{m}(X)\cong H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X,{\bf R}^{1}\epsilon_{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}))
.

Lemma 5.6
.

For
D^{\prime}\geq D
, the canonical map
C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D^{\prime})\to C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D)
is
surjective.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the cokernel of the inclusion

{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X|D^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X|D}
is a coherent sheaf
supported on
D^{\prime}
. In view of Lemma
2.2
(2), we can assume that

D^{\prime}
has the same support as that of
D
. The coherence claim then
follows from
[
17
, Prop. 1.1.9]
.
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

The change of topology map induces a short exact sequence

0\to C_{m}(X,D)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{X|D}}C_{m}^{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,D)\to T_{m}(X)\to 0.

Proof.

Using the Leray spectral sequence associated to

\epsilon
and Corollary
5.3
, we get an exact sequence

(5.3)

0\to C_{m}(X,D)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{X|D}}C_{m}^{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,D)\to H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X,{\bf R}^{1}\epsilon_{*}({\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X|D}))\to 0.

It suffices therefore to show that the canonical map

{\bf R}^{1}\epsilon_{*}({\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X|D})\to{\bf R}^{1}\epsilon_{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})
is
bijective. Equivalently,
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X|D})\to H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})
is bijective for every
x\in X
.
Clearly, it suffices to show this bijection when
x\in D
.
We shall in fact show that both these groups are zero in this case.

We first note that the exact sequence

(5.4)

0\to{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X^{h}_{x}|D^{h}_{x}}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{2,X^{h}_{x}}/{p^{m}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{x}\to 0

on
(X^{h}_{x})_{\text{\'{e}t}}
yields an exact sequence

(5.5)

{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}/{p^{m}}\to H^{0}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{G}}_{x})\to H^{1}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{2,X^{h}_{x}|D^{h}_{x}})\to H^{1}(X^{h}_{x},{{\mathcal{K}}_{2,X^{h}_{x}}}/{p^{m}}).

Since (
5.4
) is exact also in the Nisnevich
topology, we get

H^{0}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{G}}_{x})\cong H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{G}}_{x})=\frac{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}{\kappa^{m}_{2}(A^{h}_{x}|n_{x})+p^{m}K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}
. It follows that the left arrow in
(
5.5
) is surjective.
We can now conclude the proof using Lemma
2.3
.
∎

5.3.
Fundamental group with modulus

Let
D=\sum_{x}n_{x}[x]\in{\rm Div}(X)
be an effective divisor with complement
U
.
Let
D_{\dagger}=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\}
be the support of
D
with the reduced closed
subscheme structure.
We let

(5.6)

{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})={\rm Ker}\left(H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\to{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right).

(5.7)

{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})={\rm Ker}\left(H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})\to{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})}\right).

Note that
{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,A)\cong H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X,A)
for

A\in\{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\}
.
As a consequence of Proposition
2.8
, we get the following.

Corollary 5.8
.

All maps in the commutative diagram

(5.8)

\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})\bigcap H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{~{}_{p^{m}}{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})\bigcap H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}

are bijections.

Definition 5.9
.

We let
{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}(K)=H^{1}(U)\{p^{\prime}\}\bigoplus{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})
and
\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Ab}}({\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}(K),{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}})
. We shall consider

{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}(K)
as a discrete topological abelian group so that

\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)\cong({\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}(K))^{\star}
is a profinite topological
abelian group.

By
[
9
, Lem. 2.8]
, there are canonical quotient homomorphisms
of profinite groups

(5.9)

\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(K)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab,{\rm t}}_{1}(U)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X),

where
\pi^{\rm ab,{\rm t}}_{1}(U)
is the abelianized tame étale fundamental group of

U
(cf.
[
9
, § 2]
). Furthermore, an argument similar to that
of
[
8
, Thm. 7.17]
shows that
\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
is the
automorphism group of the fiber functor associated to the Galois category
consisting of finite abelian covers of
U
whose logarithmic ramifications (in the
sense of Abbes-Saito
[
1
]
) at a point
x\in D_{\dagger}
are bounded by

n_{x}-1
(cf.
[
8
, Defn. 7.2]
).

By combining Corollary
5.8
with
[
23
, Lem. 2.2]
, we obtain the
following.

Corollary 5.10
.

We have topological isomorphisms

H^{1}(U)={\underset{{\rm supp}\,(D)=D_{\dagger}}{\bigcup}}\ {\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}(K)\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(U,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})={\underset{{\rm supp}\,(D)=D_{\dagger}}{\bigcup}}\ {\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}_{{\text{\'{e}t}}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}).

5.4.
Reciprocity homomorphism

We keep the notations of §
5.3
.
For
x\in U_{(0)}
, let
\rho^{x}_{X|D}
denote the composite map

k(x)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\rho_{k(x)}}G_{k(x)}\xrightarrow{(\iota_{x})_{*}}\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
, where
\rho_{k(x)}
is the classical
reciprocity for the local field
k(x)
.
For
x\in D_{\dagger}
, we have the composite map

\rho^{x}_{X|D}\colon K_{2}({K}^{h}_{x})\xrightarrow{\rho_{{K}^{h}_{x}}}G_{{K}^{h}_{x}}\xrightarrow{(j_{{K}^{h}_{x}})_{*}}\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
,
where
\rho_{{K}^{h}_{x}}
is defined in §
2.4
.
Taking the sum over
x\in U_{(0)}
and

x\in D_{\dagger}
, we get continuous homomorphisms

\rho_{U}\colon F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F_{2}(U)\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)
and

\rho_{X|D}\colon F_{1}(U)\bigoplus F_{2}(X|D)\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
.
It is shown in
[
13
, § 3]
that
\rho_{U}
and
\rho_{X|D}

factor through compatible continuous homomorphisms

(5.10)

\rho_{U}\colon C(U)\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U);\ \ \ \rho_{X|D}\colon C(X,D)\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D).

Corollary
5.10
implies that

\rho_{U}
factors through
C(U)\to C^{c}(U)\to\widehat{C}(U)\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)
.
Let
\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)_{0}
denote the kernel of the composite map

\pi_{X|D}\colon\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X)\xrightarrow{\pi_{X}}G_{k}
, where
\pi_{X}
is the push-forward map induced by the
structure map
X\to{\rm Spec\,}(k)
. We let
C(U)_{0}
(resp.
\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)_{0}
) denote
the kernel of the composite map
C(U)\to C(X)\to k^{\times}
(resp.

\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X)\to G_{k}
). One defines
C(X,D)_{0}
and

\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)_{0}
similarly.

Corollary 5.11
.

There are commutative diagrams

(5.11)

\textstyle{C(X,D)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{X|D}}
\textstyle{C(X,D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N_{X|D}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{X|D}}
\textstyle{k^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{k}}
\textstyle{C(U)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{U}}
\textstyle{C(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{U}}
\textstyle{k^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{k}}
\textstyle{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{X|D}}
\textstyle{G_{k}}
\textstyle{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{U}}
\textstyle{G_{k}.}

Proof.

We only need to show that the right squares in both diagrams are commutative.
But this is a simple consequence of our constructions and

[
20
, § 3.1, Cor. 1, Cor. 2]
.
∎

6.
Logarithmic Hodge-Witt cohomology with
modulus

Our goal in the next two sections is to prove Theorem
1.5
.
In this section, we shall
construct a pairing between the étale cohomology of
logarithmic Hodge-Witt sheaves with modulus (cf. Proposition
6.11
).
We begin by recalling some definitions.

6.1.
Twisted de Rham-Witt sheaves

Let
X
be a connected regular
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-scheme of dimension one and let
E\subset X

be a reduced effective divisor on
X
. For a divisor

D={\underset{x\in E}{\sum}}n_{x}[x]
on
X
with support
E
,
we let
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
be the Nisnevich
(resp. étale) sheaf on
X
which is locally defined as

W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)=[f^{-1}]\cdot W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\subset j_{*}(W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})
,
where
[-]\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is the Teichmüller function,

f\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is a local equation of
D
, and
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
is the
inclusion of the complement of
E
in
X
. One knows that
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)

is a sheaf of invertible
W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules. We let
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(D)=W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}\otimes_{W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\subset j_{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{U})
.

We let
W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D}=W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)(-D)
, where

W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}({\operatorname{log}}E)
is the logarithmic de-Rham-Witt complex
à la Hyodo-Kato
[
14
]
. Let
Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}={\rm Image}(F\colon W_{m+1}\Omega^{r}_{X}\to W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X})={\rm Ker}(F^{m-1}d\colon W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}\to\Omega^{r+1}_{X})
.
Let
Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}(D)=j_{*}(Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{U})\cap W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}(D)

and
Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D}=j_{*}(Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{U})\cap W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D}
.
By
[
7
, Prop. 3.16]
, there is a Cartier map

C\colon Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}(pD)\to W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}(D)

which is surjective and whose kernel is
dV^{m-1}\Omega^{r-1}_{X}(p^{m}D)
.
Furthermore,
C
is the unique morphism such that
{\bf{p}}\circ C=V
, where

{\bf{p}}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}(D)\hookrightarrow W_{m+1}\Omega^{r}_{X}(D)
is a monomorphism such that

{\bf{p}}\circ R
is multiplication by
p
(cf.
[
46
, Prop. 1.1.4]
,

[
7
, Lem. 3.14]
).
Let
\overline{F}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}\to\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}}{dV^{m-1}\Omega^{r-1}_{X}}
be
the absolute Frobenius (cf. (
2.8
))
such that
\pi\circ F=\overline{F}\circ R
, where

\pi\colon{W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}}\to\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}}{dV^{m-1}\Omega^{r-1}_{X}}
is the
quotient map.

Assume now that
D
is effective and let
K
denote the function field of
X
.
For
x\in X_{(0)}
, let
\iota^{x}\colon{\rm Spec\,}({K}^{h}_{x})\to X
be the canonical morphism. We let
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
be
the Nisnevich (resp. étale) sheaf on
X
defined by

(6.1)

\begin{array}[]{lll}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}&=&{\rm Ker}\left(j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\to\bigoplus_{x\in E}\par\frac{\iota^{x}_{*}W_{m}({K}^{h}_{x})}{\iota^{x}_{*}{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}W_{m}({K}^{h}_{x})}\right)\\
&=&{\rm Ker}\left(j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\to\bigoplus_{x\in E}\par\iota^{x}_{*}(\frac{W_{m}({K}^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}W_{m}({K}^{h}_{x})})\right).\end{array}

We let
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}=j_{*}(Z_{1}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U})\cap{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
Since
C\colon Z_{1}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
is given by

C(a^{p}_{m-1},\ldots,a^{p}_{0})=(a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{0})
,
it follows that
C
preserves
Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and one gets a
morphism
C\colon Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
The following is easy to verify using the formula

[f]\underline{a}=(fa_{m-1},f^{p}a_{m-2},\ldots,f^{p^{m-1}}a_{0})\in W_{m}(K)
.

Lemma 6.1
.

We have the identity
{\rm fil}_{D}W_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}=W_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
. In general, we have

{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\subseteq W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)={\rm fil}_{p^{m-1}D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}.

6.2.
Complexes of sheaves and their pairings

For the rest of §
6
, we fix a local field
k
of characteristic

p>0
and a connected, smooth and projective curve
X
over
k
.
We let
K
be the function field of
X
. We also fix a positive integer
m
.
Let
D_{\dagger}=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\}
be a
reduced effective Cartier divisor on
X
and let
X^{o}\subset X
be
the complement of
D_{\dagger}
. For
\underline{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{s})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{s}
, we let
D_{\underline{n}}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}[x_{i}]\in{\rm Div}(X)
. For
\underline{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{s}
and
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we let

\underline{n}_{+}=\underline{n}+\underline{1}:=(n_{1}+1,\ldots,n_{s}+1),\ \underline{n}_{-}=\underline{n}-\underline{1}:=(n_{1}-1,\ldots,n_{s}-1),\ q{\underline{n}}=(qn_{1},\ldots,qn_{s})
and

\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle=p^{m-1}{\underline{n}}
.
We shall say that
{\underline{n}}\geq{\underline{n}}^{\prime}
if
n_{i}\geq n^{\prime}_{i}
for each
i
.
We shall write
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
if
n_{i}\geq 0
for each
i
.
For any divisor
D=\sum_{y}m_{y}[y]
on
X
, we let

D/p=\sum_{y}\lfloor{m_{y}}/p\rfloor[y]
.
We let
{{\underline{n}}}/p=(\lfloor{n_{1}}/p\rfloor,\ldots,\lfloor{n_{r}}/p\rfloor)
.

For
r\geq 0
and
\underline{n}\geq 0
, we consider the following complexes
in
D_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X)
.

(6.2)

W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{r}=\left[Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}\xrightarrow{1-C}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}\right];\ \ W_{m}{\mathcal{M}}^{r}_{\underline{-n}}=\left[Z_{1}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\underline{-n}}}\xrightarrow{1-C}W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\underline{-n}}}\right].

(6.3)

W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r}=\left[W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}\xrightarrow{1-\overline{F}}\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X}}{dV^{m-1}\Omega^{r-1}_{X}}\right];\ \ W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r}_{\underline{n}}=\left[W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}\xrightarrow{1-\overline{F}}\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}}{dV^{m-1}\Omega^{r-1}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}}}\right].

(6.4)

W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}=\left[W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X}\xrightarrow{1-C}W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X}\right]\cong W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}.

The differential graded algebra structure of
W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(p^{i}D)

induces a commutative diagram of cup product pairings of complexes
(cf.
[
17
, Lem. 3.1.1]
)

(6.5)

\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r}\times W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{2-r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\times W_{m}{\mathcal{M}}^{2-r}_{-{\underline{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}.}

By
[
31
, Prop. 4.4]
(see also
[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
),
there is a bijective trace homomorphism

{\rm Tr}_{X}\colon H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
.
Using this trace map, (
6.5
)
gives rise to a commutative diagram of cohomology pairings

(6.6)

\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2-i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{2-r})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}_{+}})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2-i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{M}}^{2-r}_{-{\underline{n}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.}

Since the canonical map
H^{2}_{x}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\to H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})

is an isomorphism for any
x\in X_{(0)}
(cf.
[
31
, Prop. 4.4]
),
(
6.5
) also yields a commutative diagram of cohomology pairings
(cf.
[
46
, § 3.1]
)

(6.7)

\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2-i}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{2-r})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}_{+}})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2-i}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{M}}^{2-r}_{-{\underline{n}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},}

which is compatible with (
6.6
).

In order to prove our duality theorem,
we need to modify the bottom complexes in (
6.5
) and the induced
cohomology pairings. We first claim the following.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
, there is a canonical exact sequence of étale sheaves

(6.8)

0\to W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}\xrightarrow{1-\overline{F}}\frac{W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}}{dV^{m-1}\Omega^{r-1}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}}}.

Proof.

The lemma follows by comparing (
6.8
) with the known case
{\underline{n}}=0

and using the proof of
[
17
, Lem. 2.3.1]
if we show that

(1-{F})(W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}})=0
.
The latter statement follows from the stronger claim that

(1-{F})(W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})=0
. But this can be easily checked
(cf.
[
32
, Prop. 2.6]
).
∎

Corollary 6.3
.

We can replace
W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}_{+}}
by
W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}

in (
6.5
).

We next consider the complex

W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}}=\left[Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\xrightarrow{1-C}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right]
for any effective divisor
D\subset X
.
We let
W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}}=W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X|D_{\underline{n}},{\operatorname{log}}}
for
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
.
It follows from Lemma
6.1
that there
is a term-wise isomorphism of complexes
W_{m}{\mathcal{M}}^{0}_{-\underline{n}}\xrightarrow{\cong}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}
. In particular, we get a commutative diagram
of cup product pairings of complexes

(6.9)

\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\ \ \times\ \ W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}\times W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}.}

For any
x\in X_{(0)}
, let
\tau^{h}_{x}\colon{\rm Spec\,}(k(x))\hookrightarrow X^{h}_{x}
and

\tau_{x}\colon{\rm Spec\,}(k(x))\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion maps, and let

j_{x}\colon X^{h}_{x}\to X
be the canonical étale map.
Recall (cf.
[
46
, § 3.1]
) that any pairing of complexes of sheaves

{\mathcal{A}}^{\cdot}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}^{\cdot}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\cdot}
on
X_{\text{\'{e}t}}
gives rise to a pairing

\tau^{*}_{x}{\mathcal{A}}^{\cdot}\otimes^{L}\tau^{!}{\mathcal{B}}^{\cdot}\to\tau^{!}{\mathcal{C}}^{\cdot}
in
D_{\text{\'{e}t}}(k(x))
. Applying this to (
6.9
),
we get a commutative diagram of pairings

(6.10)

\textstyle{\tau^{*}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\ \ \times\ \ \tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}_{x}W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}\times\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}}

in
D_{\text{\'{e}t}}(k(x))
which is compatible with (
6.9
).

6.3.
Relation with local duality

For any

{\underline{n}}\geq 0
, we let
W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}}
be defined by the short exact sequence

(6.11)

0\to W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X|D_{{\underline{n}}},{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{m}\Omega^{r}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}}\to 0

of Nisnevich (resp. étale) sheaves on
X
. We can write

W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}}=\ {\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}(\tau_{x})_{*}\circ(\tau_{x})^{*}(W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}})
. In particular, the proper base change theorem for the
torsion étale sheaves implies that

H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}})={\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}})
.
We let
W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}}=\left[\frac{Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{Z_{1}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}\xrightarrow{1-C}\frac{{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}\right]
so that we have a
term-wise short exact sequence of complexes on
X_{\rm nis}
(resp.
X_{\text{\'{e}t}}
):

(6.12)

0\to W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}}\to 0.

By definition of the pairing between complexes of sheaves (cf.

[
34
, § 1, p. 175]
), the pairing on the top row of (
6.9
)
is equivalent to a morphism of complexes
W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\to{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})
.
Composing this with the canonical map
{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})\to{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})
, we get a morphism
\theta\colon W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\cong W_{m}{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\to{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})
in
D_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X)
.
By
[
43
, Prop. 6.6]
,
this is equivalent to a morphism
\theta^{\prime}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\otimes^{L}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}
in
D_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X)
. Similarly, the bottom row of
(
6.9
) gives rise to
a morphism in the derived category

\theta_{\underline{n}}\colon W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}\to{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})
. Moreover, we have a
commutative diagram

(6.13)

\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{{\underline{n}}}}
\textstyle{{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}).}

Letting
\psi_{\underline{n}}
denote the induced map between the homotopy fibers, we
get a commutative diagram of distinguished triangles in
D_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
:

(6.14)

\textstyle{W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0},W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
.

Applying
{\bf R}{\rm Hom}^{\cdot}({{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},(-))
to the above diagram, and using the
canonical isomorphism
{\bf R}{\rm Hom}^{\cdot}({{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{B}}^{\cdot},{\mathcal{C}}^{\cdot}))\xrightarrow{\cong}{\bf R}{\rm Hom}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{B}}^{\cdot},{\mathcal{C}}^{\cdot})
(cf.
[

, Prop. 6.6]
)
as well as the canonical map

{\bf R}^{i}{\rm Hom}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{B}}^{\cdot},{\mathcal{C}}^{\cdot})\to{\rm Hom}({\bf R}^{j}{\rm Hom}({{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},{\mathcal{B}}^{\cdot}),{\bf R}^{i+j}{\rm Hom}({{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},{\mathcal{C}}^{\cdot}))
,
we get a commutative diagram of long exact hypercohomology sequences

(6.15)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where we have used the shorthand

for

. The exactness of the bottom row at the left end
is a consequence of Lemma
6.8
.

If
x\in D_{\dagger}
, then (
6.10
) gives rise to a commutative diagram

(6.16)

\textstyle{\tau^{*}_{x}W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{{\underline{n}},x}}
\textstyle{{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}_{x}W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{x}}
\textstyle{{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0},\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{H}}),}

which is compatible with (
6.13
).

Since
\tau^{*}_{x}
is exact, it is easy to check that the homotopy fibers of
the left and the right vertical arrows are
\tau^{*}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle}[-1]

and
{\bf R}{\mathcal{H}{om}}^{\cdot}(\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle},\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{H}})
, respectively. We also note that the maps

j^{*}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle}\to(\tau^{h}_{x})_{*}\tau^{*}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle}

and
(\tau^{h}_{x})_{*}\tau^{!}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}\to j^{*}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}

are isomorphisms on the étale (and Nisnevich) site of
X^{h}_{x}
.
Since the canonical (i.e., forget support) map

H^{2}_{x}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\to H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})
is bijective,
letting
\psi_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle,x}
denote the induced map between the mapping cones
of the vertical arrows in (
6.16
), considering the
corresponding maps between the hypercohomology and using the proper base change,
we obtain a commutative diagram

(6.17)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}}
\textstyle{H^{0}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}_{x}}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\tau^{*}_{x}W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle,x}}
\textstyle{G^{1}(x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle,x}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

of étale cohomology on
X^{h}_{x}
whose rows are exact and which is compatible with
(
6.15
). Here, we let

G^{i}(x)=H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x}|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}})
.

6.4.
The maps
\psi_{\underline{n}}
vs.
\widetilde{\rho}^{{\underline{n}}}_{K,m}

Our goal now is to compute the cohomology of
W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}}
and
W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\underline{n}}
,
and show that
\psi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle,x}
coincides with the reciprocity map

\widetilde{\rho}^{n_{x}+1}_{K,m}
of Corollary
4.6
.
We write
D_{\underline{n}}={\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\amalg}}D_{{\underline{n}},x}
.

Lemma 6.4
.

For
x\in D_{\underline{n}}
, we have the following.

(1)

H^{i}_{x}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{\underline{n}})\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{\underline{n}})
.

(2)

H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}&\mbox{if $r=0$}\\
{{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}(D_{{\underline{n}},x})}/{p^{m}}&\mbox{if $r=1$}\\
\varpi^{m}_{r}(A^{h}_{x}|n_{x})&\mbox{if $r\geq 2$}.\end{array}\right.

(3)

H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}})=0
for
i\geq 1
and
r\geq 2
.

Proof.

The items (1) and (2) are clear from the definition of
W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}}
while
(3) follows from
[
17
, Prop. 1.1.9]
which says
that
W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{r}_{{\underline{n}}}
has a finite decreasing filtration whose graded quotients
are coherent sheaves on
D_{\underline{n}}
.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

For
x\in D_{\dagger}
, we have
H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=0
for
i\geq 1
.
In particular,

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}&\mbox{if $i=0$}\\
{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})&\mbox{if $i=1$}\\
0&\mbox{if $i>1$.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

One deduces from (
2.10
) that there is a commutative diagram
of exact sequences

(6.18)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D/p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D/{p^{2}}}W_{m-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{m-1}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
\overline{F}
is injective and
Z_{1}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}={\rm Image}(F\colon W_{m+1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})={\rm Image}(\overline{F}\colon W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
,
it follows that the sequence

(6.19)

0\to Z_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\xrightarrow{R}Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D/p}W_{m-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to 0

is exact on
X_{\text{\'{e}t}}
.

Since
Z_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\cong(\psi_{*}Z_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})(D/p)
, it follows
that
Z_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
is a coherent sheaf on
X
. In particular,

H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},Z_{1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=0
for all
i\geq 1
. Using induction on
m
and
the long exact cohomology sequence associated to (
6.19
),
we get
H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=0
for
i\geq 1
.
Similarly, the bottom row of (
6.18
) implies that

H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=0
for
i\geq 1
.
It follows that
{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})=0
for
i>1

and
{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})
is the cokernel of the map

H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\to H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
.

Now, we note that

H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}(K^{h}_{x})
and

H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X})

={\rm fil}_{n_{x}}W_{m}(K^{h}_{x})
.
Since
(1-\overline{F})({\rm fil}_{\lfloor{n_{x}}/{p}\rfloor}W_{m}(K^{h}_{x}))

=(1-C)(Z_{1}{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}W_{m}(K^{h}_{x}))
, it follows from
(
2.11
) and the first statement of the lemma that

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\cong{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
.
It remains to show that
{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\cong{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
.
But this follows because, in the sequence of canonical maps

(6.20)

{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}\cong{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}},

the composite map is an isomorphism. This concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.6
.

We have

{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\cong\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})};\ \ \ {\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{x}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})=0\ \ \mbox{for}\ \ i\neq 2.

Proof.

Follows immediately from Lemma
6.5
using the localization
sequence

\cdots\to{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{x}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{i+1}_{x}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\to\cdots.

∎

Using (
6.18
), (
6.19
) and the fact that the
operators
V
and
C
commute with each other on
j_{*}W_{m}{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{o}}
, we get the
following.

Corollary 6.7
.

There is an exact sequence of complexes

0\to{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}}\xrightarrow{V^{m-1}}W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}}\xrightarrow{R}W_{m-1}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{{{\underline{n}}}/p}\to 0.

Lemma 6.8
.

We have

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\frac{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}&\mbox{if $i=1$}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Since

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}})\cong{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}})
, we can replace
X
by
X^{h}_{x}

in order to prove the lemma. We now consider the long exact sequence

(6.21)

\cdots\to{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})\xrightarrow{\alpha_{0}}{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}})\hskip 85.35826pt

\hskip 256.0748pt\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}}\cdots.

It follows from (
6.20
)
that
\alpha_{0}
is a bijection between two groups, each isomorphic to

{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
.
Lemma
6.5
implies that
\alpha_{1}
is injective. It follows that

{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}})=0
. We now apply Lemma
6.5
and
(
6.21
) to conclude the proof.
∎

Proposition 6.9
.

For every
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{\rm fil}_{D_{{\underline{n}}_{+}}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}}).

Proof.

Using Corollary
6.6
, we see that the
long exact sequence

\cdots\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{D_{\dagger}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\xrightarrow{j^{*}}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{D_{\dagger}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}}).

is of the form

(6.22)

0\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})\xrightarrow{j^{*}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\to{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\frac{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}.

We can now use (
5.6
) to conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

The diagram

(6.23)

\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\varpi^{m}_{2}(A^{h}_{x}|\langle n_{x}+1\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{\langle n_{x}+1\rangle-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{\langle n_{x}\rangle}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\vee}}

commutes, where the right vertical arrow on the top is the direct sum of

and on the bottom is the direct sum of
canonical surjections, taken over

.

Proof.

The diagram (
6.23
) is the composition of
two squares

(6.24)

\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus\ \psi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle,x}}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\varpi^{m}_{2}(A^{h}_{x}|\langle n_{x}+1\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{\langle n_{x}\rangle}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\vee},}

where
\theta^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}
is the composite right vertical arrow in
(
6.23
).
Since (
6.15
) and (
6.17
) are compatible, it follows
that the left square in (
6.24
) is commutative.
It remains to show that the right square commutes.
We can therefore assume
D_{\dagger}=\{x\}
. We write
\underline{n}=n
.

Now, it follows from Lemma
6.4
that the map

{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}/{p^{m}}\cong H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\to H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle n\rangle})
is surjective.
Corollary
6.6
(with
{\underline{n}}=0
) and
Lemma
6.8
imply that

{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})^{\vee}\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle n\rangle})^{\vee}

is also surjective. We conclude that (
6.17
) breaks into
a commutative diagram of short exact sequences

(6.25)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x}|D_{\langle{n+1}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}_{\langle{n}\rangle}}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}_{x}}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{n+1}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle n\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle n\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

By definition of Kato’s reciprocity map (cf. §
2.4
), it follows
from Corollary
6.6
that
the left square in (

) is the same as the left square in
the commutative diagram

(6.26)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\kappa^{m}_{2}(A^{h}_{x}|\langle n+1\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\rho}^{\langle n+1\rangle}_{K^{h}_{x},m}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{(\langle n+1\rangle)}_{K^{h}_{x},m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{(0)}_{K^{h}_{x},m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{\langle n\rangle}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})}\right)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

From this, it immediately follows that

\psi^{*}_{\langle{n}\rangle,x}=\theta^{*}_{\langle n\rangle}
.
∎

The following is the main cohomology pairing of this section that we shall use in
§
7
to prove the duality theorem for
X^{o}
.

Proposition 6.11
.

For
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
, the bottom row of (
6.9
) induces a
cohomology pairing

(6.27)

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})\times H^{2-i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}},{\operatorname{log}}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.

Proof.

We look at the diagram

(6.28)

\textstyle{H^{0}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{H^{1}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{{\underline{n}}_{+}}\rangle},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
