\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{0}(W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle_{+}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}}

\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{2}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}(W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}(W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0})^{\vee}}

of étale cohomology on
X
whose rows are exact and the curved arrow in the
middle is the map
\theta^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}
.
Note that
\psi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}
has a factorization as
indicated in the diagram because it is simply Kato’s reciprocity map
as shown in Lemma
6.10
.

To prove the proposition for
i=1
is to show that

\theta^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}
factors through
\phi^{*}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle}
such that all squares in the
lower floor commute. But this is a straightforward diagram chase and we omit
the details.
The
i=0
case is clear because neither
{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})
nor

H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}},{\operatorname{log}}})
depends on
{\underline{n}}
, as one can easily
deduce from Lemmas

and

.

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Since the three rows are exact and the two squares on the top floor as well as

shows that there exists

which factorizes

and

∎

7.
Duality theorem for open curves

We fix a local field
k
of characteristic
p>0
and a connected, smooth and
projective curve
X
over
k
. We also fix a positive integer
m
.
We let
D_{\dagger}=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\}
be a reduced effective divisor on
X

and let
j\colon X^{o}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the complement of
D
.
In this section, we shall prove a duality theorem for the
p
-adic étale
cohomology on
X^{o}
. We shall maintain the notations of §
6.2
.
For
{\underline{n}},i\geq 0
, we let
G^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)=H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D_{{\underline{n}}},{\operatorname{log}}})

and
F^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)={\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})
. Note that

G^{i}_{0}(m)=H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})
and

F^{i}_{0}(m)={\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\underline{n}})=H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
for
{\underline{n}}\leq 0
.

7.1.
Topology of
G^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)
in a special case

We shall endow each
G^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)

with the structure of a topological abelian group with some restriction on
{\underline{n}}

when
i=1
.
It is clear that
G^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)=0
for
i\geq 3

(cf.
[
35
, Chap. VI, Rem. 1.5]
). As for
i=2
, we have seen in the
proof of Proposition
6.11
that
G^{2}_{\underline{n}}(m)
does not depend on

{\underline{n}}
, as is the case with
F^{0}_{\underline{n}}(m)
.
On the other hand, the pairing (
6.5
) induces an isomorphism

G^{2}_{0}(m)\xrightarrow{\cong}F^{0}_{0}(m)^{\vee}\cong{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
by

[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
(see also
[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
).
We therefore endow
G^{2}_{\underline{n}}(m)
with the discrete
topology via this isomorphism.

For
i=0
, we note that the commutative squares on the lower floor of
(
6.29
) exist for any
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
. It follows then from
Corollary
4.6
, Lemma
6.10
, (
6.29
) and

[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
(see also
[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
) that
the map
G^{0}_{\underline{n}}(m)\to F^{2}_{{\underline{n}}_{-}}(m)^{\vee}
is an isomorphism.
Since
F^{2}_{0}(m)
has discrete topology (cf.
[
31
, Prop. 3.6]
) and

F^{2}_{{\underline{n}}_{-}}(m)
is its quotient,
the latter is a discrete topological abelian group. We therefore endow

G^{0}_{\underline{n}}(m)
with the profinite topology so that

(7.1)

G^{0}_{\underline{n}}(m)\times F^{2}_{{\underline{n}}_{-}}(m)\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}

is a perfect pairing of topological abelian groups.

To define the topology on
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m)
, we shall use a recipe.
Let
G
and
G^{\prime}
be two abelian groups such that
G^{\prime}
is a torsion group. Let

G^{\prime}\times G\to{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}
be a pairing which has no left kernel. Let

\beta\colon G^{\prime}\to G^{\vee}
be the induced injective homomorphism.
We let
G
be endowed with the weakest topology
with respect to which the map
\beta(f)\colon G\to{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}
is continuous
for every
f\in G^{\prime}
(called the weak topology induced by
G^{\prime}
).
This makes
G
a topological abelian group for which
the identity element has a fundamental system of open neighborhoods of the form

{\rm Ker}(\beta(f_{1}))\cap\cdots\cap{\rm Ker}(\beta(f_{r}))
with

f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}\in G^{\prime}
.
It is clear that
\beta
has a factorization
\beta\colon G^{\prime}\hookrightarrow G^{\star}
, where
the dual on the right is taken with respect to the weak topology.

Lemma 7.1
.

The map
\beta\colon G^{\prime}\to G^{\star}
is a bijection.

Proof.

Let
f\in G^{\star}
. We can find
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}\in G^{\prime}
such that
the continuous homomorphism
f\colon G\to{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}
has the property that

H:=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\cap}}}{\rm Ker}(\beta(f_{i}))\subset{\rm Ker}(f)
.
We now look at

(7.2)

\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{G}/H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{G}{{\rm Ker}(f)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{f}}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
\textstyle{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}\frac{G}{{\rm Ker}(\beta(f_{i}))}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}

Since
{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}
is injective, there exists
\widetilde{f}
such that the above diagram
is commutative. Since
G^{\prime}
is a torsion group, we see that each summand of the
the group on the bottom is a finite cyclic group.
It follows that we can replace
{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}}
by a finite cyclic group
{{\mathbb{Z}}}/q

in (
7.2
). Using the standard description of homomorphisms between
finite cyclic groups, we can find integers
n_{1},\ldots,n_{r}
such
that
f=\widetilde{f}\circ\Delta=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}\beta(f_{i})=\beta(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}f_{i})\in G^{\prime}
.
∎

For a topological abelian group
G
whose topology is
\tau
, we let
G^{{\rm pf}}

denote the profinite completion of
G
with respect to
\tau
. That is,
G^{{\rm pf}}
is
the inverse limit
{\varprojlim}_{U}\ {G}/U
with the inverse limit
topology, where
U
runs through the
\tau
-open subgroups of finite index in
G
.
It is clear that
G^{{\rm pf}}
is a profinite abelian group and the
profinite completion map
G\to G^{{\rm pf}}
is continuous with dense image.
By the Pontryagin duality, this implies the following.

Lemma 7.2
.

If
G
is a torsion abelian group, then the map
(G^{{\rm pf}})^{\star}\to G^{\star}
is a
topological isomorphism for the discrete topology of
G^{\star}
.

Let
G
be a torsion topological abelian group and let
G^{\star}
be endowed with
the discrete topology. Then the evaluation map
{\rm ev}_{G}\colon G\to(G^{\star})^{\vee}
is continuous with respect to the profinite topology of the target
group. This implies that
{\rm ev}_{G}
has a canonical factorization

G\to G^{{\rm pf}}\xrightarrow{{\rm ev}^{{\rm pf}}_{G}}(G^{\star})^{\vee}
with
{\rm ev}^{{\rm pf}}_{G}

continuous.

Corollary 7.3
.

The map
{\rm ev}^{{\rm pf}}_{G}\colon G^{{\rm pf}}\to(G^{\star})^{\vee}
is a topological
isomorphism.

Proof.

Follows from Lemma
7.2
using the Pontryagin duality.
∎

To apply the above recipe, we use Proposition
6.11
to
endow
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)
with the structure of a
topological abelian group via the weak topology induced by
F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m)
.
To check that the hypothesis of Lemma
7.1
is satisfied,
we look at the commutative diagram

(7.3)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{1}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\theta^{*})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\psi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{F^{2}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\theta^{*})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{0}(m)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\vee}}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle_{+}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{0}_{0}(m)^{\vee},}

obtained by dualizing the lower floor of (
6.28
).
Lemmas
6.4
and
6.8
imply that
the rows of this diagram are exact. In combination with
Proposition
4.7
, Lemma
6.10
and
[
25
, Prop. 3.4]

(see also
[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
), these results also imply that

(\phi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}
is a monomorphism. This proves that the desired hypothesis
is satisfied.

We conclude from Lemma
7.1

that with respect to the weak topology of
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)
,
the map
(\phi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\colon F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m)\to G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)^{\star}
is a
bijection. We thus get a commutative diagram

(7.4)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{1}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\theta^{*})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\psi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{F^{2}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\theta^{*})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{0}(m)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\vee}}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle_{+}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{0}_{0}(m)^{\star}}

whose rows are exact and the vertical arrows are bijections, where

H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle{{\underline{n}}}\rangle_{+}})
is endowed with the Kato topology.

Remark 7.4
.

Via the pairing
F^{1}_{0}(m)\times G^{1}_{0}(m)\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
, we can endow

G^{1}_{0}(m)
with the weak topology induced by

F^{1}_{0}(m)
. Using the Pontryagin duality between the
profinite topology of
G^{1}_{0}(m)
and the discrete
topology of
F^{1}_{0}(m)
(cf.
[
31
, Thm 4.7]
),
it is easy to check that

\{{\rm Ker}(\beta(f))|f\in F^{1}_{0}(m)\}
forms a sub-base for
the profinite topology of
G^{1}_{0}(m)
. It follows that
the weak topology of
G^{1}_{0}(m)
coincides with the
profinite topology. Furthermore,
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)\to G^{1}_{0}(m)

is continuous.

Lemma 7.5
.

The profinite completion map
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)\to(G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}

is injective, and the induced subspace topology of
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)

coincides with its weak topology. In particular, the latter topology is
Hausdorff.

Proof.

By Corollary
7.3
, the injectivity claim is equivalent to that

{\rm ev}_{G}\colon G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)\to((G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{\star})^{\vee}
is
injective.
By (
7.4
), the latter claim is equivalent to that the map

\phi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}
in (
6.28
) is injective.
But this follows because
\psi^{*}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}
is injective by
Corollary
4.6
and Lemma
6.10
while the maps
\theta^{*}

are bijective by
[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
(see also

[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
). The claim, that the weak topology of
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)

coincides with the subspace
topology induced from the profinite topology of
(G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{\rm pf}
,
follows from Lemma
7.1
whose proof shows that every open subgroup of

G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)
has finite index.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

For
{\underline{n}}\geq{\underline{n}}^{\prime}
, the canonical map
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)\to G^{1}_{\langle n^{\prime}\rangle_{+}}(m)
is
continuous and the induced map
(G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{\rm pf}\to(G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}^{\prime}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{\rm pf}

is a topological quotient.

Proof.

We look at the commutative diagram

(7.5)

\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle n^{\prime}\rangle_{+}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{\rm pf}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}^{\prime}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{\rm pf}.}

Lemma
6.10
implies that
the transition map
{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}^{\prime}\rangle})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})
is injective.
By comparing the exact sequence in the top row of (
7.3
) with the
similar exact sequence for
\langle{\underline{n}}^{\prime}\rangle
, we see that the transition map

F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}^{\prime}\rangle}(m)\to F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m)
is injective.
We conclude from (
7.4
) and Corollary
7.3
that
the bottom horizontal arrow in (
7.5
) is a surjective continuous
homomorphism between two profinite groups. It must therefore be a topological
quotient map. Finally, the claim, that the horizontal arrow in the top row of
(
7.5
) is continuous, follows from Lemma
7.5
which
asserts that each term of this row is a subspace of the corresponding term in the
bottom row.
∎

Remark 7.7
.

It is unclear that the transition map
G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)\twoheadrightarrow G^{1}_{\langle n^{\prime}\rangle_{+}}(m)

is a quotient map in general.

By combining Corollary
7.3
, (
7.4
) and the Pontryagin
duality, we get the following special case of the duality theorem. We let

{\underline{n}}\geq 0
.

Corollary 7.8
.

For
i\geq 0
, there is a perfect pairing of
topological abelian groups

(G^{i}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}\times F^{2-i}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m)\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.

7.2.
Topology of
G^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)
in general case

To endow
G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
with the structure of a topological abelian group in general,
we fix
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
and let
\phi({\underline{n}})=(m_{1},\cdots,m_{r})
be such that

m_{i}
is the smallest non-negative integer having the property that

p^{m-1}m_{i}+1\geq n_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq r
. In particular, the canonical map

G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)\to G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
is surjective by Lemma
5.6
. We endow

G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
with the quotient of the weak topology of
G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)
via this
surjection. This makes
G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
into a topological abelian group.

Lemma 7.9
.

For
{\underline{n}}\geq{\underline{n}}^{\prime}
, the transition map
\gamma^{\underline{n}}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}\colon G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)\to G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m)
is continuous and the induced map

(G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}}(m))^{\rm pf}\to(G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m))^{\rm pf}
is a topological quotient.

Proof.

By definition of
\phi({\underline{n}})
, there is a commutative diagram

(7.6)

\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}}^{\prime})\rangle_{+}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m).}

vertical arrows are quotient maps. It follows that the bottom horizontal arrow
is also continuous. For the second part, we note that any quotient of

G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
by an open subgroup is also a quotient of
G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)
by an
open subgroup. It follows therefore from
[
38
, Lem. 1.1.5]
that
the map
(G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}\to(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{\rm pf}
is surjective.
We now use the commutative diagram obtained by taking the profinite completions of
the groups appearing in (
7.6
) and use Lemma
7.6
to
conclude that the map
(G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}}(m))^{\rm pf}\to(G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m))^{\rm pf}
is a topological
quotient.
∎

Lemma 7.10
.

For every
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
, the topological abelian group
G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
is Hausdorff.

Proof.

We shall prove the equivalent statement that the kernel of the transition map

\alpha_{\underline{n}}\colon G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)\twoheadrightarrow G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
is closed. By
the commutative diagram

(7.7)

\textstyle{G^{0}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{G^{0}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\underline{n}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

whose rows are exact,
it suffices to show that
\partial_{0}({\rm Ker}(\beta_{\underline{n}}))
is closed in

G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)
.

Now, we look at the commutative diagram of continuous homomorphisms

(7.8)

\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{{\beta_{\underline{n}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\underline{n}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}_{{\underline{n}}_{-}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}_{-}})^{\vee}.}

The horizontal arrows in this diagram are injective by Corollary
4.6
and
Lemma
6.10
. This implies that

(7.9)

{\rm Ker}(\beta_{\underline{n}})=(\psi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle})^{-1}({\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}})).

In particular,
{\rm Ker}(\beta_{\underline{n}})
is closed in
H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}})
.

We next consider the commutative diagram

(7.10)

\textstyle{G^{0}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{0}}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{0}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{(F^{0}_{0}(m))^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}}
\textstyle{(F^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}(m))^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(F^{1}_{0}(m))^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}
is a continuous homomorphism between profinite
abelian groups and
{\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}})
is closed in

{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}})^{\vee}
, it follows that

\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}({\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}}))
is closed in

(F^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}(m))^{\vee}
. In particular,

(\phi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle})^{-1}(\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}({\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}}))
is closed in

G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)
. It remains therefore to show that

(7.11)

\partial_{0}({\rm Ker}(\beta_{\underline{n}}))=(\phi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle})^{-1}(\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}({\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}})).

Let
x\in(\phi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle})^{-1}(\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}({\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}}))

so that
\phi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}(x)=\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}(y)
for some

y\in{\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}})
. By a diagram chase of (
7.10
), we get

a\in H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}})
and

b\in G^{0}_{0}(m)
such that
x=\partial_{0}(a)
and

\psi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}(a-\delta(b))=y\in{\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}})
.
It follows from (
7.9
) that

a^{\prime}:=a-\delta(b)\in{\rm Ker}(\beta_{\underline{n}})
and
x=\partial_{0}(a)=\partial_{0}(a^{\prime})
. This shows that

(\phi^{*}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle})^{-1}(\gamma^{\vee}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle}({\rm Ker}(\beta^{\prime}_{\underline{n}}))\subseteq\partial_{0}({\rm Ker}(\beta_{\underline{n}}))
. The reverse inclusion follows by (
7.8
).
∎

Corollary 7.11
.

For every
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
, the profinite completion map

G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)\to(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}
is
injective and the topology of
G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
coincides with the subspace topology
induced from the profinite topology of
(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}
.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
7.10
, it suffices to show that every open subgroup
of
G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
has finite index. But this is clear because this property
holds for
G^{1}_{\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}}(m)
whose topological quotient is
G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)
.
∎

7.3.
Duality for
X^{o}

We let
G^{i}_{m}({X^{o}})={\varprojlim}_{{\underline{n}}}(G^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}
and endow it with the
structure of a profinite abelian group by means of the inverse limit topology.
Note that the canonical map
{\varprojlim}_{{\underline{n}}}G^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)\to G^{i}_{m}({X^{o}})
is a
topological
isomorphism for
i\neq 1
. We let
H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
have the discrete
topology for each
i\geq 0
. We end §
7
with the following duality
theorem for
X^{o}
. This extends the duality theorem of Kato-Saito
(cf.
[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
) to open curves.

Theorem 7.12
.

For each
i\geq 0
, there is a perfect pairing of topological abelian
groups

(7.12)

G^{i}_{m}({X^{o}})\times H^{2-i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.

Proof.

First of all, it follows easily by a combination of Lemma
6.1

and the argument given at the outset of
[
17
, § 4]
that there is a
canonical isomorphism

{\varinjlim}_{\underline{n}}F^{i}_{\underline{n}}(m)\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
.
Now, the
i=0
case of the theorem follows directly from (
7.1
),
which says that the duality statement in fact holds for every
{\underline{n}}
. One can also
check using the ampleness of
D_{\underline{n}}
that
F^{2}_{\underline{n}}(1)=0
for
{\underline{n}}\gg 0
. Using
Corollary
6.7
and induction on
m
, one gets that

F^{2}_{\underline{n}}(m)=0=G^{0}_{\underline{n}}(m)
for
{\underline{n}}\gg 0
.
As shown in §
7.1
, the
i=2
case is independent of
{\underline{n}}
and the
duality statement holds by
[
25
, Prop. 3.4]
(see also

[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
).

To prove
i=1
case, we first note that there are topological isomorphisms

G^{1}_{m}({X^{o}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}G^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)
and

{\varinjlim}_{\underline{n}}F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}(m)\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
.
We now combine Corollary
7.8
,
[
7
, Lem. 5.2]

and
[
31
, Lem. 2.6]
to conclude the proof.
∎

Remark 7.13
.

If we let
H^{1}_{c,{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X^{o},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}}):=H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,j_{!}W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})
, we can endow

H^{1}_{c,{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X^{o},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})
with
the weak topology induced by
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
via the pairing

H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\times H^{1}_{c,{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X^{o},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}
. It can be shown that the
canonical map
H^{1}_{c,{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X^{o},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})\to G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})
induces an
isomorphism of topological abelian groups
H^{1}_{c,{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X^{o},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})^{\rm pf}\xrightarrow{\cong}G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})
. In particular,
(
7.12
) for
i=1
can be written as

H^{1}_{c,{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X^{o},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})^{\rm pf}\times H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.

8.
Class field theory with modulus

We fix a local field
k
of characteristic
p>0
and a connected, smooth and
projective curve
X
over
k
.
We let
D_{\dagger}=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\}
be a reduced effective divisor on
X

and let
j\colon X^{o}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the complement of
D
.
Let
K
denote the function field of
X
.
In this section, we shall prove a general form of Corollary
7.8

and derive the class field of
X
with modulus.
We shall continue to maintain the notations of
§
6.2
and §
7
.

8.1.
Duality theorem with modulus

We fix an integer
m\geq 1
. We let
\psi(n)=p^{m-1}n+1
for
n\geq 0
.
We let
\rho^{m}_{X^{o}}\colon\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\to\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}
and
\rho^{m}_{X|D}\colon C_{m}(X,D)\to\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)}{p^{m}}
be the continuous homomorphisms
induced by the reciprocity maps of (
5.10
).

We let
\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X^{o}}\colon G^{1}_{m}({X^{o}})\to\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}

and
\lambda^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X^{o}}\colon\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\to G^{1}_{m}({X^{o}})

be the continuous homomorphisms induced by the perfect pairing of
(
7.12
) via the identification

H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{\vee}\cong\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}
.
We consider the sequence of homomorphisms

(8.1)

\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\to{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}\ C_{m}(X,D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}})\hookrightarrow{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}\ C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}})\hookrightarrow G^{1}_{m}({X^{o}})\xrightarrow{\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X^{o}}}\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}.

The two arrows in the middle are injective by
Proposition
5.7
and Corollary
7.11
.
We let
\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X^{o}}
denote the composite homomorphism.

Lemma 8.1
.

We have
\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X^{o}}=\rho^{m}_{X^{o}}
.

Proof.

We let
x\in D_{\dagger}
and look at the diagrams

(8.2)

\textstyle{\frac{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{K^{h}_{x}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}H^{1}_{x,\ {\rm nis}}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}H^{1}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}C_{m}(X,D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}(F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m))^{\vee}}

and

(8.3)

\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}(F^{1}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle}(m))^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{\vee}.}

By (
) and Lemma
6.5
, the map

H^{1}_{x}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{K}}^{m}_{X|D_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle_{+}}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle})^{\vee}

in the middle row of (
8.2
)
coincides (for a fixed
\langle{\underline{n}}\rangle
) with the
composition of Kato’s reciprocity homomorphism
with the projection
\frac{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x}|\psi(n_{x}))+p^{m}K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}\to({\rm fil}_{n_{x}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}))^{\vee}
. In particular, the top square in
(
8.2
) commutes. The bottom square clearly commutes because
it is the local-global comparison square for the cohomology pairing of
Proposition
6.11
. The diagram (
8.3
) is easily seen to be
commutative.

The composition of the horizontal arrow on the top of
(
8.2
) and the left vertical arrow in (
8.3
)
is
\rho_{K^{h}_{x}}
. On the other hand, the composition of the horizontal rows on the
bottom of (
8.2
) and the right vertical arrow in
(
8.3
) is
\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X^{o}}
.
It follows that
\rho^{m}_{X^{o}}
and
\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X^{o}}
coincide when
restricted to
\frac{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}{p^{m}}
. A similar and easier argument shows that

\rho^{m}_{X^{o}}
and
\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X^{o}}
coincide when
restricted to
\frac{K_{1}(k(x))}{p^{m}}
if
x\in X^{o}_{(0)}
. As

F_{1}(X^{o})\bigoplus F_{2}(X^{o})\twoheadrightarrow C(X^{o})
, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

For any
{\underline{n}}\geq 1
, we have a Cartesian square

(8.4)

\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{p^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{(\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}})^{\vee}.}

Proof.

Using the commutative diagram

(8.5)

\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{(\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}({K}^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{m}_{\widehat{K}_{x}}}
\textstyle{(\frac{K_{2}({K}^{h}_{x})}{p^{m}})^{\vee}}

for every
x\in D_{\dagger}
, the lemma is easily deduced from

[
23
, Rem. 6.6]
and Corollary
5.8
.
∎

Let
\nu_{\underline{n}}\colon C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})\to C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})
denote the étale
realization map.

Lemma 8.3
.

For any
{\underline{n}}\geq 1
, we have a Cartesian square

(8.6)

\textstyle{((G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{(\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}})^{\vee}.}

Proof.

We let
{\underline{n}}^{\prime}=\langle\phi({\underline{n}})\rangle_{+}
and observe that
(
8.6
) has a factorization

(8.7)

\textstyle{((G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{((G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m))^{{\rm pf}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\vee}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\vee}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\vee}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{(\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}})^{\vee},}

where the left horizontal arrows are obtained by dualizing the transition
maps.

We claim that the left square in (
8.7
)
is Cartesian. To show this, we can replace
(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}

(resp.
(G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}
) by
C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})
(resp.
C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})
)
by Lemma
7.2
. By dualizing the exact sequence of
Proposition
5.7
, it already follows now that the left square in
(
8.7
) is Cartesian if we use

(-)^{\vee}
instead of
(-)^{\star}
in its top row. The claim now follows because

C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})\twoheadrightarrow C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})
is a topological quotient map.

To show that the right square in (
8.7
)
is Cartesian, we can replace
((G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m))^{{\rm pf}})^{\star}
and
G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})^{\star}

by
{\rm fil}_{D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
and

H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{0},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})
, respectively, by Proposition
6.9
,
Corollary
7.8
and Theorem
7.12
.
We can now apply Lemma
8.2
to finish the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

The map
\rho^{{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{X^{o}}\colon G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})\to\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}
induces a continuous homomorphism

\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}\colon C^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})\to\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{\underline{n}})}{p^{m}}

for every
{\underline{n}}\geq 0
such that

(8.8)

\textstyle{G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{X^{o}}}
\scriptstyle{q_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}}
\textstyle{\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{\underline{n}})}{p^{m}}}

is a commutative diagram of continuous homomorphisms between topological abelian
groups. Moreover,
\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}
is a topological isomorphism.

Proof.

We shall assume
{\underline{n}}\geq 1
as the lemma is clear otherwise.
For the first part, we only need to show the existence of
\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}

such that (
8.8
) commutes as the continuity follows from
Lemma
7.9
.
We now look at the diagram

(8.9)

\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\vee}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{m}(X^{o}))^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\vee}}
\textstyle{(\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}})^{\vee}.}

The outer square is commutative by the compatibility between

\rho_{X^{o}}
and
\rho_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}
,
and
\nu^{\vee}\circ(\rho^{{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}=(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}
by
Lemma
8.1
.
By Lemma
8.3
, a diagram chase yields
a continuous homomorphism

(\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})^{\star}\colon{\rm fil}_{D_{\underline{n}}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\to(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{{\rm pf}})^{\star}
such that the left square in (
8.9
)
commutes.
Letting
\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}
to be the dual this map, we conclude the proof of the
first part of the lemma.
For the second part, we only need to show that

(\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})^{\star}
is surjective. But this follows
by Lemmas
8.2
and
8.3
.
∎

The following stronger version of Corollary
7.8

proves Theorem
1.5
. For
D=\emptyset
, this was shown by Kato-Saito

[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
by a different method.

Theorem 8.5
.

For any effective divisor
D\subset X
and
i\geq 0
,
there is a perfect pairing of topological abelian groups

H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})^{\rm pf}\times H^{2-i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})\to{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.

such that for
D^{\prime}\geq D
, one has a commutative diagram

(8.10)

\textstyle{H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})^{{\rm pf}}\times H^{2-i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D^{\prime},{\operatorname{log}}})^{{\rm pf}}\times H^{2-i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X|D^{\prime},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}}.}

Proof.

For
i\neq 1
, this is already shown in the proof of Theorem
7.12
.
For
i=1
, the existence of the pairing and its perfectness follow from
Lemma
8.4
. To prove the commutativity of (
8.10
),
we can assume
D^{\prime}=D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}
and
D=D_{\underline{n}}
for some
{\underline{n}}^{\prime}\geq{\underline{n}}
and
look at the diagram

(8.11)

\textstyle{G^{1}_{m}(X^{o})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{{\text{\'{e}t}}}_{X^{o}}}
\scriptstyle{q_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{q_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}_{{\underline{n}}}}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{{\underline{n}}}(m))^{{\rm pf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{{\underline{n}}}}}
\textstyle{\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{p_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}_{{\underline{n}}}}
\textstyle{\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}})}{p^{m}}.}

Since the left and the outer squares commute by Lemma
8.4
and

q_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}
is surjective by Lemma
7.9
,
it follows using Proposition
6.9
that the right square
commutes.
∎

Remark 8.6
.

Using (
7.10
) and the proof of Proposition
4.10
,
one can show that the canonical map
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})\to H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})^{\vee}
is not surjective if
D\neq\emptyset
.
This implies that no matter what topology we endow

H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X|D,{\operatorname{log}}})
with, Theorem
8.5
will not hold
without taking the profinite completion.

8.2.
Proofs of parts (1)
\sim
(3) of
Theorem
1.1

We need two more lemmas in order to prove
parts (1) and (2) of Theorem
1.1
.

Lemma 8.7
.

For every
m,{\underline{n}}\geq 1
, the composite map

\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}\colon C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})\xrightarrow{\nu_{\underline{n}}}G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)\hookrightarrow(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{\rm pf}\xrightarrow{\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}}\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}})}{p^{m}}

coincides with
\rho^{m}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}
.

Proof.

We look at the diagram

(8.12)

\textstyle{\frac{C(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}\ C_{m}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}\ G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{m}({X^{o}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X^{o}}}
\scriptstyle{q_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(G^{1}_{\underline{n}}(m))^{\rm pf}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}}
\textstyle{\frac{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}})}{p^{m}}.}

Note that the composite horizontal arrow on the top is
\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X^{o}}
.
Since
s_{\underline{n}}
is surjective, it suffices to show that

\rho^{m}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}\circ s_{\underline{n}}=p_{\underline{n}}\circ\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X^{o}}
.
But this follows directly from Lemma
8.1
,
Lemma
8.4
and the compatibility between
\rho_{X^{o}}
and
\rho_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}
.
∎

Let
T^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
denote the kernel of the canonical
surjection
\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)\twoheadrightarrow\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X)
. Let

\rho^{0}_{X|D}\colon C(X,D)_{0}\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)_{0}
be the geometric part of
the reciprocity map
\rho_{X|D}
.

Lemma 8.8
.

T^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
is a torsion group of finite exponent. In particular,

{\rm Image}(\rho^{0}_{X|D})
is a torsion group of finite exponent.

Proof.

The second part of the lemma follows directly from its first part by comparing

\rho^{0}_{X|D}
with
\rho^{0}_{X}
since the image of the latter map is finite
by
[
40
]
and
[
45
]
(see also
[
9
, Thm. 1.4]
).
It remains to prove the first part of the lemma.
We let
D=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\underset{i=1}{\sum}}}n_{i}[x_{i}]
.

By (
2.9
) and
[
7
, Lem. 8.7]
,
we have an inclusion

\frac{{\rm fil}_{D}H^{1}(K)}{H^{1}(X)}\hookrightarrow\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}\frac{{\rm fil}_{n_{i}-1}H^{1}({K}^{h}_{x_{i}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}({K}^{h}_{x_{i}})}
.
Equivalently,
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}\left(\frac{{\rm fil}_{n_{i}-1}H^{1}({K}^{h}_{x_{i}})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}({K}^{h}_{x_{i}})}\right)^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow T^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
.
Hence, it suffices to show that for every
x\in D_{\dagger}
and integer
n\geq 0
,

\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}({K}^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}({K}^{h}_{x})}
has
finite exponent. By Lemma
2.3
and the definition of Kato’s
ramification filtration, we can replace
K^{h}_{x}
by
\widehat{K}_{x}
.

Now,
\frac{{\rm fil}_{n}H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})}
is annihilated by
some power of
p
by
[
23
, Cor. 3.3]
and

\frac{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})}\cong\frac{H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})\{p^{\prime}\}}{H^{1}(\widehat{A}_{x})\{p^{\prime}\}}

by (
2.9
).
On the other hand, the localization sequence and purity theorem for
the étale cohomology imply that there is an inclusion

\frac{H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})\{p^{\prime}\}}{H^{1}(\widehat{A}_{x})\{p^{\prime}\}}\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{0}(k(x))\{p^{\prime}\}\cong(\mu_{\infty}(k(x)))^{\vee}
.
But the last term is finite (of order prime to
p
).
It follows that
\frac{{\rm fil}_{0}H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{-1}H^{1}(\widehat{K}_{x})}

is finite, and this clearly implies the lemma.
∎

The following result extends the class field theory
of Kato-Saito
[
25
, § 3]
and Hiranouchi

[
13
]
to the general case of ramified class field theory.

Theorem 8.9
.

For every effective divisor
D\subset X
and every integer
n\geq 1
, the
reciprocity homomorphism

{\rho}_{X|D}\colon{C(X,D)}/n\to{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)}/n

is injective.

Proof.

If
p\nmid n
, then the right hand side can be replaced by

{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}/n
by Definition
5.9
and

[
9
, Lem. 2.8]
.
On the other hand, the left hand side can be replaced by
{C(X^{o})}/n
.
The corollary now follows from
[
13
, Thm. 1.1]
(see also

[
9
, Thm. 1.2]
). We can therefore assume
n=p^{m}
for some
m\geq 1

and
D=D_{\underline{n}}
for some
{\underline{n}}\geq 1
.
By Lemma
8.7
, it suffices in this case to show that

\widetilde{\rho}^{m}_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}
is injective. But this follows by combining
Proposition
5.7
, Corollary
7.11
and Lemma
8.4
.
∎

Theorem 8.10
.

For every effective divisor
D\subset X
, the kernel of the
reciprocity map

{\rho}_{X|D}\colon{C(X,D)}\to{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)}

is the maximal divisible subgroup of
C(X,D)
.

Proof.

Given Theorem
8.9
and Lemma
8.8
, we can
repeat the proof of
[
40
, Thm. 5.1]
. We omit the
details.
∎

The following result proves part (3) of Theorem
1.1
.

Proposition 8.11
.

The image of the map
\rho_{X|D}\colon C(X,D)_{0}\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)_{0}

is not necessarily finite.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the image of the map

\rho_{X|D}\colon{C(X,D)_{0}}/p\to{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)_{0}}/p
may not be
finite. Using Theorem
8.9
and the inclusion

{C(X,D)_{0}}/p\hookrightarrow{C(X,D)}/p
, it suffices to show that

{C(X,D)_{0}}/p
may not be finite. It further suffices to show that

{\rm Ker}(\theta_{X|D}\colon{C(X,D)}/p\to{C(X)}/p)
may not be finite.

We now take
X={\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{k},\ D_{\dagger}=\{\infty\}
and
D=2D_{\dagger}
. By
(
2.2
) and Corollary
5.3
, we have have an exact sequence

(8.13)

H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\to\varpi^{1}_{2}(A^{h}_{\infty}|2)\to{\rm Ker}(\theta_{X|D})\to 0.

By the duality, we have
H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\cong H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\cong H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/p)^{\star}=0
.
It remains therefore to show that
\varpi^{1}_{2}(A^{h}_{\infty}|2)
is infinite.
But this follows from
[
19
, § 2, Prop. 1]
and Lemma
2.2

which imply that
\varpi^{1}_{2}(A^{h}_{\infty}|2)
is an infinite-dimensional

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-vector space.
∎

9.
Class field theory of
{X^{o}}

We maintain the notations of §
8
. We shall prove Theorem
1.3

and prove parts of Theorem
1.2
in this section.

9.1.
Proofs of parts (1)
\sim
(3) of
Theorem
1.2

In this subsection, we shall prove the first three parts of Theorem
1.2
.
We begin with the injectivity result.

Theorem 9.1
.

For every integer
n\geq 1
, the map

{\rho_{X^{o}}}\colon{C(X^{o})}/n\to{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}/n
is injective.

Proof.

If
p\nmid n
, then the projection maps
{C(X^{o})}/n\to{C(X,D_{\dagger})}/n
are isomorphisms by
[
9
, Cor. 5.10]
.
On the other hand, it is clear that the map
{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}/n\to{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{\dagger})}/n
is an isomorphism. The injectivity of

{\rho_{X^{o}}}/n
now follows from Theorem
8.9
.

We now let
n=p^{m}
and
{\mathcal{F}}=j_{!}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})
. We have an exact sequence

(9.1)

{K_{2}(K)}/{p^{m}}\to{\underset{x\in X_{(0)}}{\bigoplus}}H^{1}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\to H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{F}}).

If
x\in X^{o}
, then
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x},{\operatorname{log}}})=0

(cf. the proof of Proposition
5.7
). This implies

H^{1}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\cong H^{1}_{x,{\rm nis}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\cong H^{1}_{x,{\rm nis}}(X^{o},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})\cong{k(x)^{\times}}/{p^{m}}
.
If
x\in D_{\dagger}
, then we have an exact sequence

(9.2)

0\to H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{F}})\to H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{F}})\to H^{1}_{x,{\text{\'{e}t}}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\to H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{F}}).

In the exact sequence (where
\iota_{x}\colon{\rm Spec\,}(k(x))\hookrightarrow X^{h}_{x}
is the
inclusion) of étale cohomology

H^{0}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x},{\operatorname{log}}})\to H^{0}(k(x),\iota^{*}_{x}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x},{\operatorname{log}}}))\to H^{1}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{F}})\to H^{1}(X^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{h}_{x},{\operatorname{log}}}),

the first arrow from the left is bijective by the proper base change theorem
and the last group was observed above to be zero.
It follows that
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{F}})=0
.
Since
H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{F}})
is clearly zero and
H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},{\mathcal{F}})\cong H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K^{h}_{x},W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{K^{h}_{x},{\operatorname{log}}})
, we conclude that
the canonical map
{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}/{p^{m}}\to H^{1}_{x}(X,{\mathcal{F}})
is an isomorphism.
It follows that the left arrow in (
9.1
)
is the map
{K_{2}(K)}/{p^{m}}\xrightarrow{(\partial_{X^{o}},\vartheta_{X^{o}})}{F_{1}(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}\bigoplus{F_{2}(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}
.

Since

{K_{2}(K)}/{p^{m}}\to{\underset{x\in X_{(0)}}{\bigoplus}}H^{1}_{x,{\rm nis}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\to H^{1}_{\rm nis}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\to 0

is exact and the Nisnevich analogue of (
9.2
) implies that

{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}/{p^{m}}\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{1}_{x,{\rm nis}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})
for
x\in D_{\dagger}
,
we conclude that there is a canonical isomorphism

(9.3)

H^{1}_{\rm nis}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{C(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}.

In particular, we have an inclusion
{C(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})
.

We now let
\iota\colon D_{\dagger}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion and consider the
commutative diagram of pairings in
D_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X)
:

(9.4)

\textstyle{\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\times\iota_{*}\iota^{!}(W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\times W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{!}(W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})\times{\bf R}j_{*}(W_{m}\Omega^{0}_{X^{o},{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}}.}

Considering the induced pairings of étale cohomology, we get a commutative
diagram of exact sequences

(9.5)

\textstyle{H^{0}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}\frac{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}{p^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{1}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}_{X^{o}}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(X,W_{m}\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}H^{2}_{x}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{2}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{\vee}.}

It is clear from the pairing in the top row of (
9.4
) that
the second vertical arrow from the left in the above diagram is the direct
sum of
\rho_{A^{h}_{x}}
. Furthermore, the
commutativity of (
9.4
) and the definition of
the right arrow
\partial^{\prime}_{X|D}
in Lemma
5.2
imply that

\partial_{1}
coincides with the restriction of the canonical map

{F_{2}(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}\to{C(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}
in the definition of
C(X^{o})
.
Similarly,
\partial_{2}
coincides with the sum of
the canonical maps
{G^{(0)}_{K^{h}_{x}}/{p^{m}}}\to{G_{K}}/{p^{m}}\to{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}
. It follows that the composition

{C(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}\hookrightarrow H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{F}})\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}_{X^{o}}}{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}
is
the reciprocity map
\rho^{m}_{X^{o}}
. We thus have to show that
\rho^{\prime}_{X^{o}}
is
injective.

Now, we note that the vertical arrows on the left and the right ends of
(
9.5
) are isomorphisms by
[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
.
A diagrams chase shows that it suffices to show that for every
x\in D_{\dagger}
,
the reciprocity map

\rho_{A^{h}_{x}}\colon\frac{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}{p^{m}}\to{G^{(0)}_{K^{h}_{x}}}/{p^{m}}
is injective.
But this follows by Corollary
4.2
.
The proof concludes.
∎

Corollary 9.2
.

The kernel of
\rho_{X^{o}}\colon C(X^{o})\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})

is the maximal divisible subgroup of
C(X^{o})
.

Proof.

The argument for the prime-to-
p
divisibility of
{\rm Ker}(\rho_{X^{o}})
is identical
to that of
[
13
, Thm. 4.6]
while the
p
-divisibility
follows from Lemma
2.12
and Theorem
9.1
.
∎

The following result proves part (3) of Theorem
1.2
.

Proposition 9.3
.

The image of
\rho_{X^{o}}\colon C(X^{o})_{0}\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})_{0}

is not necessarily finite.

Proof.

Using Theorem
9.1
in place of Theorem
8.9

and repeating the proof of Proposition
8.11
, it suffices to show
that
{\rm Ker}(\theta_{X^{o}}\colon{C(X^{o})}/p\to{C(X)}/p)
may not be finite.
Taking
X={\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{k}
and
D_{\dagger}=\{\infty\}
, we see from
(
9.3
) that there is an exact sequence

(9.6)

0\to H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X,{\operatorname{log}}})\to\Omega^{2}_{A^{h}_{\infty},{\operatorname{log}}}\to{\rm Ker}(\theta_{X^{o}})\to 0.

As in the proof of Proposition
8.11
, one reduces to showing that

{K_{2}(A^{h}_{\infty})}/p\cong\Omega^{2}_{A^{h}_{\infty},{\operatorname{log}}}
is infinite. But this follows
from
[
19
, § 2, Prop. 1]
, Lemma
2.2
and
Lemma
2.3
, which together imply
that
{K_{2}(A^{h}_{\infty})}/p
has an infinite decreasing filtration
whose graded quotients are infinite-dimensional
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-vector spaces.
∎

9.2.
Reciprocity for
\widehat{C}(X^{o})

We observed in §
5.4
that
\rho_{X^{o}}
factors through the continuous
homomorphism
\widehat{\rho}_{X^{o}}\colon\widehat{C}(X^{o})\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})
,
where
\widehat{\rho}_{X^{o}}={\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}\rho_{X|D_{\underline{n}}}
.
We shall now prove Theorem
1.3
.
First, we prove some lemmas.

Lemma 9.4
.

The pro-abelian group
\{_{p^{m}}C(X,D_{\underline{n}})\}_{{\underline{n}}}
is zero for every integer

m\geq 1
.

Proof.

By an induction argument, it suffices to prove the lemma for
m=1
.
By (
), we have an exact sequence

(9.7)

G^{0}_{\underline{n}}(1)\to{K_{2}(K)}/{p}\to{F_{1}(X^{o})}/p\bigoplus{F_{2}(X|D_{\underline{n}})}/p\to{C(X,D_{\underline{n}})}/p\to 0.

By Lemma
7.5
and Corollary
7.8
, we have

G^{0}_{\underline{n}}(1)\hookrightarrow G^{0}_{\underline{n}}(1)^{\rm pf}\cong F^{2}_{\underline{n}}(1)
for
{\underline{n}}\gg 0
.
On the other hand, we observed in the proof of Theorem
7.12
that

F^{2}_{\underline{n}}(1)=0
for
{\underline{n}}\gg 0
. We conclude that there is an exact sequence
of pro-abelian groups

(9.8)

0\to{K_{2}(K)}/{p}\to{F_{1}(X^{o})}/p\bigoplus\{{F_{2}(X|D_{\underline{n}})}/p\}_{\underline{n}}\to\{{C(X,D_{\underline{n}})}/p\}_{n}\to 0.

Together with Lemma
2.1
, this implies that
in the sequence of canonical maps of pro-abelian groups

\bigoplus_{x\in D_{\dagger}}\{_{p}(K_{2}({k(x)[t]}/{(t^{n_{x}})}))\}_{{\underline{n}}}\to\bigoplus_{x\in D_{\dagger}}\{_{p}({K_{2}(K^{h}_{x}))}/{{\rm fil}_{n_{x}}K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})})\}_{{\underline{n}}}\to\{_{p}C(X,D_{\underline{n}})\}_{{\underline{n}}}
, the first arrow is an isomorphism and
the second arrow is levelwise surjective.
It suffices therefore to show that
\{_{p}K_{2}(A_{n},(t))\}_{n}=0
if

A_{n}={k^{\prime}[t]}/{(t^{n})}
for a local field
k^{\prime}
of characteristic
p
.
But this follows from
[
6
, Lem. 5.1, 5.7]
.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

For
m\geq 1
, there is an exact sequence

(9.9)

0\to{\widehat{C}(X^{o})}\xrightarrow{p^{m}}{\widehat{C}(X^{o})}\to{\varprojlim}_{\underline{n}}{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})}\to 0.

Proof.

This follows by taking the limits in the exact sequence of
pro-abelian groups

(9.10)

0\to\{_{p^{m}}C(X,D_{\underline{n}})\}\to\{C(X,D_{\underline{n}})\}\xrightarrow{p^{m}}\{C(X,D_{\underline{n}})\}\to\{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})\}\to 0

and using Lemma
9.4

∎

Lemma 9.6
.

For a prime-to-
p
integer
q
, the canonical map

{\widehat{C}(X^{o})}/q\to\varprojlim_{\underline{n}}{C(X,D_{\underline{n}})}/q
is bijective.

Proof.

Since the map
{C(X^{o})}/q\to{C(X,D_{\underline{n}})}/q
is bijective for every
{\underline{n}}\geq 1
,
it suffices to show that the map
{C(X^{o})}/q\to{\widehat{C}(X^{o})}/q
is surjective.
For this, it further suffices to show that the canonical map

{\varprojlim_{\underline{n}}F_{2}(X|D_{\underline{n}})}\to{F_{2}(X|D_{\dagger})}
is injective modulo
q
.
But this follows by fixing
x\in D_{\dagger}
,
taking limits in the exact sequence of pro-abelian groups

(9.11)

0\to\left\{\frac{{\rm fil}_{1}K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{n}K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}\right\}\to\left\{\frac{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{n}K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}\right\}\to\left\{\frac{K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}{{\rm fil}_{1}K_{2}(K^{h}_{x})}\right\}\to 0,

and noting that the pro-abelian group on the left is uniquely
q
-divisible.
∎

Lemma 9.7
.

The transition map
{\rm Ker}(\rho_{X|D_{n^{\prime}}})\to{\rm Ker}(\rho_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})
is surjective for
all
{\underline{n}}^{\prime}\geq{\underline{n}}\geq 1
.

Proof.

We write
\rho_{\underline{n}}=\rho_{X|D_{\underline{n}}},\ A_{\underline{n}}={\rm Ker}(\rho_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})
and look at the
commutative diagram

(9.12)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(X,D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{0}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}_{\underline{n}}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{{\underline{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(X,D_{{\underline{n}}})_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{0}_{{\underline{n}}}}
\textstyle{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{{\underline{n}}})_{0}.}

We let
x\in{\rm Ker}(\rho_{{\underline{n}}})
and let
M
be an integer which annihilates
the image of
\rho^{0}_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}
by Lemma
8.8
.
By Theorem
8.10
, we can write
x=Mx^{\prime}
for some

x^{\prime}\in{\rm Ker}(\rho_{{\underline{n}}})
. On the other hand, we can write

x^{\prime}=\delta^{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}_{\underline{n}}(y^{\prime})
for some
y^{\prime}\in C(X,D_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})_{0}
. Since

y:=My^{\prime}\in{\rm Ker}(\rho_{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}})
and
\delta^{{\underline{n}}^{\prime}}_{\underline{n}}(y)=x
, we are done.
∎

We can now prove Theorem
1.3
.

Theorem 9.8
.

For every integer
n\geq 1
, the reciprocity homomorphism

\widehat{\rho}_{X^{o}}\colon{\widehat{C}(X^{o})}/n\to{\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})}/n

is injective. In particular,
{\rm Ker}(\widehat{\rho}_{X^{o}})
is the maximal divisible
subgroup of
\widehat{C}(X^{o})
.

Proof.

The first part of the theorem follows by combining
Lemmas
9.5
,
9.6
and Theorem
8.9
.
The
p
-divisibility of

{\rm Ker}(\widehat{\rho}_{X^{o}})
follows by combining the first part of the theorem with
Lemmas
2.12
and
9.5
. If
p\nmid n
,
we let
B_{\underline{n}}={\rm Ker}(A_{\underline{n}}\twoheadrightarrow A_{\underline{1}})
and look at the commutative diagram

(9.13)

\textstyle{0\to{\varprojlim}B_{\underline{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{n}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}A_{\underline{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{n}
\textstyle{A_{\underline{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{n}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\to{\varprojlim}B_{\underline{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\varprojlim}A_{\underline{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{\underline{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Lemma
9.7
implies that the rows of this diagram are exact.
Since each
B_{\underline{n}}
is a
p
-primary group, the left vertical arrow is an
isomorphism. Since the right vertical arrow is surjective by Theorem
8.10
,
we conclude that the middle vertical arrow is surjective.
∎

10.
Dual of the reciprocity maps

We shall maintain the notations of §
8
.
We shall make an additional assumption that
X
is geometrically connected over
k
.
We let
r
denote the ‘rank of
X
over
k
’
(cf.
[
40
, Defn. 2.5, Thm. 6.2]
).
Our goal is to prove the last parts of Theorems
1.1
and

1.2
. The first result is the following.

Theorem 10.1
.

We have the following.

(1)

The canonical maps
\widehat{C}(X^{o})^{\star}_{\rm tor}\to C^{c}(X^{o})^{\star}_{\rm tor}\to C(X^{o})^{\star}_{\rm tor}

are isomorphisms.

(2)

The reciprocity map for
X^{o}
induces an exact sequence

(10.1)

0\to({{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{r}\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})^{\star}\xrightarrow{\rho^{\vee}_{X^{o}}}{C}(X^{o})^{\star}_{\rm tor}\to 0.

Proof.

The injectivity of maps in (1) follows from the fact that the maps

C(X^{o})\hookrightarrow C^{c}(X^{o})\to\widehat{C}(X^{o})
have dense images. The
surjectivity follows from (2).

To prove (2), we look at the commutative diagram

(10.2)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}H^{2}_{x}(A^{h}_{x},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho_{D_{\dagger}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\theta^{*})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({C(X)}/{p^{m}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({C(X^{o})}/{p^{m}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}({K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}/{p^{m}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{0}_{0}(m)^{\vee}.}

The top row in this diagram is exact and the bottom row is a complex which is
exact except possibly at the third term because of the exact sequence of
continuous maps

{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}/{p^{m}}\to{C(X^{o})}/{p^{m}}\to{C(X)}/{p^{m}}\to 0.

The vertical arrow on the right end of (
10.2
) is injective by

[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
(see also
[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
).
The arrow
(\rho_{D_{\dagger}})^{\vee}
is bijective by
[
19
, § 8, Thm. 2]
and

[
20
, § 3, Rem. 4]
.
A diagram chase implies that

(\rho^{m}_{X})^{\vee}
and
(\rho^{m}_{X^{o}})^{\vee}
share common kernels and
cokernels. We conclude from
[
13
, Cor. 5.1]
that there is an
exact sequence

(10.3)

0\to({{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{r}\to~_{p^{m}}(\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})^{\star})\xrightarrow{(\rho_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}~_{p^{m}}(C(X^{o}))^{\star})\to 0.

We now let
n
be an integer prime to
p
and let
\Lambda={{\mathbb{Z}}}/n
.
One checks from the definition of
C(X^{o})

that there is an exact sequence

(10.4)

{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}{K_{2}(A^{h}_{x})}/n\to{C(X^{o})}/n\to{C(X)}/n\to 0.

in which all arrows are continuous. By

[
9
, Lem. 8.4, 8.13, Prop. 8.15]
, the first arrow in this sequence
factors through
{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}{K_{2}(k(x))}/n
and gives
rise to an exact sequence

(10.5)

H^{2}(X,\Lambda(2))\to{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}H^{2}(k(x),\Lambda(2))\to{C(X^{o})}/n\to{C(X)}/n\to 0,

where
H^{i}(Y,\Lambda(j))
denotes Voevodsky’s motivic cohomology of
Y
.
The continuity of all maps in (
10.4
) is equivalent to that
the first two arrows in (
10.5
) are continuous.

We thus get a commutative diagram of exact sequences

(10.6)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{o},\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X^{o}}}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}H^{0}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(k(x),\Lambda(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{D_{\dagger}}}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({C(X)}/n)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({C(X^{o})}/n)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underset{x\in D_{\dagger}}{\bigoplus}}H^{2}(k(x),\Lambda(2))^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,\Lambda(2))^{\vee},}

in which all vertical arrows are the compositions of the Saito-Tate duality
maps (cf.
[
9
, Thm. 9.9]
) with the dual of the étale realization
maps between the Nisnevich and étale motivic cohomology.

By
[
9
, Prop. 9.12]
, the first two vertical arrows in
(
10.6
) coincide with
the dual of the reciprocity maps
\rho^{\vee}_{X}
and
\rho^{\vee}_{X^{o}}
, respectively.
The realization maps
H^{2}(Y,\Lambda(2))\to H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y,\Lambda(2))

are isomorphisms for
Y\in\{{\rm Spec\,}(k(x)),X\}
by the Beilinson-Lichtenbaum
conjecture. In particular, the last two vertical arrows in (
10.6
)
are isomorphisms. A diagram chase implies that

\rho^{\vee}_{X}
and
\rho^{\vee}_{X^{o}}
share common kernels and
cokernels. We conclude from
[
13
, Cor. 5.1]
that there is an
exact sequence

(10.7)

0\to({{\mathbb{Z}}}/n)^{r}\to~_{n}(\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X^{o})^{\star})\xrightarrow{(\rho_{X^{o}})^{\vee}}~_{n}(C(X^{o}))^{\star})\to 0.

Combining (
10.3
) and (
10.7
) and taking the
limit, we get (
10.1
). This proves (2).
∎

Theorem 10.2
.

For every effective divisor
D\subset X
, we have an
exact sequence

(10.8)

0\to({{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{r}\to\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)^{\star}\xrightarrow{\rho^{\vee}_{X|D}}C(X,D)^{\star}_{\rm tor}\to 0.

Proof.

We can assume
D=D_{\underline{n}}
for some
{\underline{n}}\geq\underline{1}
.
We fix an integer
m\geq 1
.
It follows from Corollary
7.11
and Lemma
8.7
that the realization
map
C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})\to C_{m}^{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,D_{\underline{n}})
is continuous.
By Proposition
6.9
and (
7.4
) (whose rows are exact for each

{\underline{n}}
), we therefore get a commutative diagram

(10.9)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{X})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\rm fil}_{D_{{\underline{n}}}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(K,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{m}_{\underline{n}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}_{{\underline{n}}_{-}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\psi^{*}_{\underline{n}})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\theta^{*})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X)^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{m}(X,D_{\underline{n}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\underline{n}})^{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{0}_{0}(m)^{\vee}}

in which the top row is exact and the bottom row is a complex which is exact
except possibly at
H^{0}_{\rm nis}(X,W_{m}{\mathcal{W}}^{2}_{\underline{n}})^{\star}
.

The vertical arrow on the right end is injective by

[
25
, § 3, Prop. 4]
(see also
[
31
, Thm. 4.7]
). The map

(\psi^{*}_{\underline{n}})^{\vee}
is an isomorphism by Proposition
4.7
and
Lemma
6.10
. By
[
13
, Cor. 5.1]
, Corollary
5.8
and a
diagram chase, we thus get an exact sequence

(10.10)

0\to({{\mathbb{Z}}}/{p^{m}})^{r}\to~_{p^{m}}(\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D_{\underline{n}})^{\star})\xrightarrow{(\rho_{X|D_{\underline{n}}})^{\vee}}~_{p^{m}}(C(X,D_{\underline{n}})^{\star})\to 0.

By (
10.7
) and
[
9
, Lem. 2.8, Cor. 5.10]
, we
have a similar exact sequence when we replace
p^{m}
by any integer prime to
p
.
Taking the limit over all integers, we get
(
10.8
).
∎

The following is immediate from Theorems
10.1
and

10.2
.

Corollary 10.3
.

For the reciprocity maps
\rho_{X^{o}}
and
\rho_{X|D}
, we have

{\rm Coker}_{\rm top}(\rho_{X^{o}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\rm Coker}_{\rm top}(\rho_{X|D})\xrightarrow{\cong}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{r}.

10.1.
Reciprocity for
K

We shall now study the reciprocity for the function field
K=k(X)
.
We let
C(K)
be the limit of the pro-abelian group
\{C(X,D)\}
, where

D
runs through all effective divisors of
X
. We endow
C(K)
with the
projective limit topology. It is clear that
C(K)
is topologically
isomorphic to
{\varprojlim}_{U}\widehat{C}(U)
, where the latter has the projective
limit topology as
U
runs through open
subsets of
X
. Since
G_{K}\to{\varprojlim}_{D}\pi^{\rm ab}_{1}(X,D)
is a
topological isomorphism, we get a continuous reciprocity homomorphism

\rho_{K}\colon C(K)\to G_{K}
.
We prove the following result.

Theorem 10.4
.

The reciprocity map
\rho_{K}
satisfies the following.

(1)

{\rm Ker}(\rho_{K})
is the maximal
p
-divisible subgroup of
C(K)
.

(2)

\rho^{m}_{K}\colon{C(K)}/{p^{m}}\to{G_{K}}/{p^{m}}
is injective for every

m\geq 1
.

(3)

If
X
is geometrically connected, there is an exact sequence

0\to({{\mathbb{Q}}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{r}\to(G_{K})^{\star}\to C(K)^{\star}_{\rm tor}\to 0.

Proof.

To prove (1), we use Lemma
9.5
to get an exact sequences of
pro-abelian
groups

(10.11)

0\to\{\widehat{C}(U)\}\xrightarrow{p^{m}}\{\widehat{C}(U)\}\to\{{\widehat{C}(U)}/{p^{m}}\}\to 0.

Taking the limit, we get an exact sequence

(10.12)

0\to C(K)\xrightarrow{p^{m}}C(K)\to{\varprojlim}_{U}\frac{\widehat{C}(U)}{p^{m}}.

It follows that the map
{C(K)}/{p^{m}}\to{\varprojlim}_{U}{\widehat{C}(U)}/{p^{m}}

is injective. We now apply Theorem
9.8
to conclude the proof of
(2). The item (1) follows from (2) and Lemma
2.12
.
The item (3) follows by taking limit (over
U
) of the exact sequences
(
10.1
) and applying
[
31
, Lem. 2.6]
.
∎
